# P5 O15 — Recomposition and the owner's week (2026-11-10)

> **Parent:** `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-10-27-p5-org.md` (P5), child **O15**, §"O15: Recomposition and the owner's week". Parent pin: blob `534e5d344a793894ec787fdd61ebdb447fe982cf` at master `f221bd3c2` (P5's Decision Logs 1–265; 266 lands with this document).
>
> **What binds.** O15's section in P5 is this document's design, and P5's approval covers it. Binding with it:
> - P5's parent-level acceptance, items 1 to 16, as worded there.
> - P5 §2, "Two composition roots" and "One daemon per profile", and OX1 (wire compatibility): nothing here narrows a schema that a process of another build reads.
> - The owner's two answers of 2026-11-10 (P5 Decision Log 266): when `mawsd` cannot start, MAWS opens a repair screen and runs nothing of the machine's inside the app; and the deletion is built now and reaches the three machines before the verdict.
> - The project's rules (`CLAUDE.md`): the engine is never patched (D-1); components use tokens only, light theme the default; gates run through `scripts/ci-gate.sh`.
>
> **Inputs.** Two inventories written on 2026-11-10 by research agents from master a839e6bb2 and read by the architect, committed beside this file: `2026-11-10-p5-o15-inventory-embedded-root.md` (what the app's main process still composes, every selector of it, its history) and `2026-11-10-p5-o15-inventory-evidence.md` (each acceptance item split into checkable claims, 67 in all, with the evidence on record for each). They are maps pinned to that commit. Where one disagrees with the code or with this document, the code and this document are right.
>
> **What this file holds.** The execution only. M1 and M2 are code, built by a plan executor on one branch. M2b is code as well, added on 2026-11-10 (Decision Log 6) and built by an executor of its own on a second branch. M3 to M5 are the architect's operations with the owner, the desk track and mini-ops, and no executor runs them.

## Purpose

P5 moved everything that belongs to a machine out of the app: sessions, terminals, the index of transcripts, attention, files, git, connectors and the library now run in `mawsd`, the machine daemon. `mawsd` is a background process, one per profile, started at login by a LaunchAgent for the owner's main profile and started by the app for every other profile (`src/mawsd/`). The app is a client of it. Three things remain before P5 can close: the first two were known when this document was written, and the third was found on 2026-11-10 by reading the code against M4's table before the final build was fixed (Decision Log 6).

**The app still contains a second way to run the machine.** `src/main/app.ts` can compose the same services inside Electron's main process (the *embedded shape*, P5 §2's second composition root), and it does so in three cases: a saved setting `supervisor.shape: embedded`, an environment variable `MAWS_HOST_MODE=detached` or `utility`, and, without anyone asking, whenever the main profile's LaunchAgent is absent, turned off or fails to start. In that last case MAWS looks normal and every promise of P5 is quietly off: sessions end with the app, and other machines cannot reach this one. After M1 and M2 the app has one arrangement on every profile. On the owner's Macs the difference shows in one place: when `mawsd` cannot start, MAWS opens a small window that says why, shows the daemon's last log lines and offers the repair, and it runs nothing of the machine's until `mawsd` answers. A developer sees it too: a development build on the main profile of a Mac with no installed MAWS shows that window, which names `pnpm dev:scratch`.

**P5's acceptance has never been checked as a whole on one build.** Each child proved its part when it landed, on the build of its day. The evidence inventory counts 67 claims in the 16 items: 31 have something seen on the owner's production machines, only 3 of those in full, and 36 have nothing live (11 a gate or a bench only, 10 staging only, 15 neither). After M3 to M5 every claim has a dated result on one final build, or the owner's ruling on it, written into P5's Outcomes, and the owner's verdict on the week closes P5.

**Some claims could not pass, or could not be seen, on the build as it stood.** The last sentence of P5's acceptance 7, every subscription resumed within 1 s of the network being reachable after a wake, held only by luck: nothing in the app reacted to the network's return, an attempt begun before it could hold a link for about 23 s, and the org feed could stay dead after a wake with nothing to notice it. Main's log had no file and no times, so the claim could not have been read off the machine either. The same read found smaller things: a replay's source that no log names, a tool description that tells the model the app must be open, a peer message drawn with no owner, a connection failure logged with an empty reason, and a refused start of the daemon that the repair window would have described wrongly. M2b fixes them before the final build is fixed. After it, resume after a wake is a property of the design, in the case §F states, that a gate case proves on both carriers, main writes a log file with a time on every line, and M4's rows name methods that the build can answer.

**How to see it working.** After M2, in the suites: an app launched with a daemon that cannot start opens the repair window and nothing else, and its Try Again opens the app once the daemon can start; a profile saved on the embedded shape opens on `mawsd` with every session and setting present. After M3, on each Mac: `ps` shows no engine host or pty host whose parent is MAWS, and Settings › Background supervisor has no "Run sessions under the machine daemon" switch. After M2b, in the measured group `e2e:remote-latency`: with the app's sockets gone silent and a wake of an hour injected, every open subscription of the link is answered within 1 s of the path's return, over the direct carrier and over the relay, with one upgrade and nothing lost or repeated; and `<profile>/logs/main.log` holds main's lines with their times. After M5: P5's Outcomes holds the table of 67 results and the owner's words.

## Acceptance

O15 is done when all of these hold.

1. **One arrangement.** No launch of the app, on any profile and with any environment or saved setting, opens the machine's data, takes `spine.lock` (the lock the one writer of a profile's machine files holds), or starts an engine host or a pty host from the app's main process. The suites show it by a check that the app's bundle holds no module of the services' composition, and by launches that read which process holds the lock.
2. **The repair window.** A launch on the main profile whose daemon cannot start shows one window that names the reason in a sentence, the daemon's last log lines where there are any, and the actions that apply; nothing else of MAWS opens. The retry action opens the app in the same process once the daemon starts. Closing the window quits MAWS. A profile with a private daemon is outside this: it starts as it does today, and a private daemon that fails to start, like any daemon that fails after the launch, shows where it shows today, in the supervisor's status row and Settings › Background supervisor.
3. **Nothing lost.** A profile last run on the embedded shape, with `supervisor.shape: embedded` saved, opens on `mawsd` with its sessions, projects, artifacts, library, drafts, layout and every other setting as they were, and no re-index.
4. **The acceptance as a whole.** Each of the 67 claims of P5's acceptance has, in P5's Outcomes, one of: a pass seen on the final build with its evidence; a citation of earlier evidence with the reason a new check adds nothing; or the owner's ruling, with a tracker row where something is carried.
5. **The record.** P5's Outcomes and retrospective is written; the risk row on the org root's key says what the key is; the items other children handed to O15 by name are each closed or carried; the owner's verdict (acceptance 16) is recorded in their words.
6. **Resume after a wake, and the log that shows it.** In the gate's wake case (§I), with six subscriptions on the link of which two are session streams whose cursors the engine host's ring has evicted, every subscription is answered within 1 s of the path's return: over the direct carrier, and over the relay while the direct path answers TCP and hangs above it. One upgrade is made on the carrier that came up, and no event is lost or repeated across the cut. The org feed comes back with one open, connected and its listings read within 2 s of the hub's path. The 1 s is claimed for a wake in which all of these hold (§F's list): the handshake needs no catch-up page; the link has at most six subscriptions; the round trips and `J` are inside §F's arithmetic, `J` being bounded by §L's checkpoints for a cursor in their dense region (Task 8, ruled in by Decision Log 27) and by the journal's length outside it; on the lane that is adopted, the first attempt after that lane's first probe answer succeeds; where that lane is direct, the bucket held two tokens when the lane made its attempt; and what the link writes on the new channel ahead of the `sub`s, acts and queries alike, is at most 4 KiB. None of them is about when the path returns while the window is open. A wake outside them is neither a pass nor a fail of the bound. Main's log is a file under the profile with a time on every line, written by the profile lock's holder alone, and it carries the lines M4's 7e reads (§H, §K).

## Constraints that bind every milestone

1. **`mawsd`'s side does not change.** `src/services/` (the library both roots compose), `src/mawsd/` and the engine host keep their behaviour. `detached` stays: it is the transport `mawsd` and the app's relay links use, and the engine host's own mode. Only the app's use of it to reach an engine host directly goes. M2b adds log lines to the engine host's session stream and to the endpoint (§H) and changes nothing else of either, unless its Task 8 is ruled in (§L): the stream's checkpoints then change where a journal read starts and no answer it gives.
2. **No bound moves.** OX7's limits (p50 26.45 ms, p95 42.56 ms, P5 Decision Log 33), acceptance 14's bound (99,145 KiB and 1.12 s per 600 s, Decision Log 128) and every other measured limit keep their recorded numbers. A ported test that cannot hold its limit on `mawsd` stops the work with the numbers; it is not loosened.
3. **Rollback stays possible.** Each machine keeps its previous build, cf30dafdd, beside the new one. So no stored file takes a version an older build cannot read: the client settings file stays at version 21 (a build that meets a newer version falls back to defaults and would overwrite the owner's settings), and the index is not touched.
4. **The wire is additive.** OX1: no command, event, capability or schema member that a process of another build sends or reads is removed or narrowed. If a change here has to be named in `src/shared/ipc/wire-manifest.json`, it is an addition and takes a minor.
5. **The older-`mawsd` paths stay.** Where the app answers `artifacts.export` or `delivery.send` itself because the connected `mawsd` does not advertise `machine.render` or `machine.delivery`, and where the artifact scheme serves locally before a welcome, the code serves an app that meets an older daemon, not the embedded shape. It stays, and M5 files its retirement.
6. **No Electron and no typecheck on the MacBook.** The executor runs no Electron, no e2e, no packaged build and no typecheck (`tsc`, `pnpm typecheck`, any `tsc --noEmit`, whatever the heap) on this Mac: every suite runs through `scripts/ci-gate.sh` from the branch's worktree. What may run locally is a focused run alone, one at a time: eslint on one file, vitest's node project on one test file. Lint over the repository, vitest over several files or a directory, and the unit suite are class 1a's on CI. After every commit that touches product or test TypeScript, a static-only or class 1a run comes before any run that holds the CI Mac.
7. **Tests never touch the owner's daemon.** A test or harness launch on the main profile is refused before any work, as today (`testLaunchRefusal`).
8. **The repository is public.** Evidence that shows the owner's sessions (screenshots, transcripts, logs with their content) stays on the architect's disk under `~/.local/share/maws-architect/o15-evidence/`; the record carries times, counts, builds and verdicts. Neither of the owner's email addresses is written anywhere.

## Plan of Work

M1 takes the suites off the embedded shape while the shape still exists, so M2's deletion is judged by suites that already pass on `mawsd`. M2 deletes the shape and builds what replaces its one useful effect, a launch that survives a daemon that cannot start. They land together as one pull request. M2b is built beside them on a branch of its own and lands before M3: it makes the last sentence of acceptance 7 a property of the design and gives M4 the means to observe what it checks. M3 puts the resulting master on the three machines. M4 checks P5's acceptance on it. M5 writes the record.

### M1 — No suite launches the app's embedded shape

**At its end.** Every package script, gate list and test that launched the tested build with the machine's services inside the app either launches it on `mawsd` or is gone. The embedded shape still exists in the app. Its own unit tests (`src/main/hosts/mode.test.ts`, `daemon.test.ts`) and the settings group's shape-flip case still run, and go with their code in M2.

**Touches.** `package.json`; `.github/workflows/gate.yml`, `.github/scripts/gate-plan.test.mjs`, `.github/scripts/e2e-default-specs.test.mjs` and `scripts/ci-gate-alerts.test.mjs`; `e2e/support/supervisor-scratch.ts`, `e2e/supervisor-packaged.spec.ts`, `e2e/supervisor.spec.ts`, `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`, `e2e/local-latency.spec.ts`, `e2e/daemon-idle.spec.ts`, `e2e/artifacts-store.spec.ts`, `e2e/support/connector-cold-delivery.ts` with `e2e/delivery.spec.ts`, `e2e/p1-live-keep-quit.spec.ts`, `e2e/c12-quit.spec.ts`; `src/main/lifecycle.test.ts`, `src/main/sessions/main-wiring.test.ts`, `src/main/profile-upgrade.test.ts`. The inventory's §2 lists each site.

**Decisions.**

- *Scripts.* Deleted: `e2e:c7-utility`, `e2e:supervisor`, `e2e:supervisor:packaged`, `e2e:local-latency`, `e2e:daemon-idle-baseline`, `e2e:daemon-idle-aba`, `e2e:keep-quit-live`. The scripts that already run on `mawsd` keep their names (`e2e:c7`, `e2e:supervisor-daemon`, `e2e:supervisor-daemon:packaged`, `e2e:local-latency-daemon`, the other `e2e:daemon-idle*`), so every gate list, document and tracker row that names them stays true; `verify` names the kept ones. The alternative, giving the daemon scripts the shorter names, was not taken: it renames what a dozen records cite for no gain.
- *A case that only the embedded shape has* (the quit question's keep or stop, the app as the engine host's only client) is deleted, and the report lists it with one line on what it pinned. A case both shapes have runs on `mawsd`. `e2e/support/supervisor-scratch.ts` defaults to `mawsd`.
- *Cross-build* (`e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`, the two-build check behind acceptance 13). The tested build launches on `mawsd` in every scene. The update and downgrade scenes that ran both builds embedded run both on `mawsd`, which is the crossing the three machines make at every swap; the scenes that pin the peer to the embedded shape (an old app's engine host adopted by the new `mawsd`, and back) keep the peer there, since the peer's own code decides its shape. If a scene fails on `mawsd` for a reason in the product, that is a finding: stop and report it, and do not weaken the scene.
- *The measured groups.* `e2e/daemon-idle.spec.ts` loses its embedded baseline and A-B-A legs; the bound's constants stay as recorded, with the commit that could last re-derive them (this branch's merge base) named beside them. `e2e:artifacts-perf` moves to `mawsd` with the profile's daemon and hosts ended before each cold trial, since a relaunch of the app no longer makes them cold; its first-sync, durable-acknowledgement and acknowledged-loss assertions and its limits are unchanged.
- *Cold connector delivery* (`e2e/support/connector-cold-delivery.ts`) proved that an HTTP connector's delivery completes with the app gone, by standing in for the app on the engine host's socket. On `mawsd` the daemon is the engine host's permanent client and delivery is the daemon's (O4). Re-express the case as that: the app quits and `mawsd` completes the delivery, observed the way O4's no-app cases observe. If an O4 case already asserts exactly this, delete the embedded case and name the O4 case in the report.
- *The live keep-quit probe* (`e2e/p1-live-keep-quit.spec.ts`, E11's paid probe of a kept turn across an embedded quit) is deleted with its script and never run: its subject no longer exists, and acceptance 2 is checked live in M4.
- *The two mocked app startups* (`src/main/lifecycle.test.ts`, `src/main/sessions/main-wiring.test.ts`) start the app on the daemon branch, as `src/main/daemon-root.test.ts` does. Their assertions on main's shutdown order stay. Their assertions on what main hands its own session service move to where that service is composed now, the services' or the daemon root's tests. `src/main/profile-upgrade.test.ts` keeps its data-migration coverage as a test of the services' `openSpineData`, called directly; which process owns the data is `profile-upgrade-mawsd.test.ts`'s. `test/support/o2-profile.ts` keeps writing `shape: embedded`: it is the legacy profile M2's migration case needs.
- *Gate lists.* The deleted scripts leave `CLASS1_E2E`, `HOSTED_MAC_E2E`, `MAC_VM_E2E`, `MEASURED_E2E` and the packaged job together with `gate-plan.test.mjs`'s assertions. Two more tests in the static job derive from the real workflow and `package.json` and change with them: `.github/scripts/e2e-default-specs.test.mjs` expects the deleted keep-quit spec in the default set and pins that set's count, and `scripts/ci-gate-alerts.test.mjs` asks a plan for the retired `e2e:supervisor`. The rule: after the deletions, no file under `.github/` or `scripts/` names a deleted script or spec. CI-opt has two pull requests in flight on the same files (static's checks in parallel; a docs-only check). Rebase onto master before the branch's gates and keep both sides' edits.

**Does not touch.** Any file under `src/` that is not a test. Any limit. The peer build's behaviour in cross-build.

**Proves.** `--gates all` is green on M1's head with the job set less the deleted groups, and each ported measured group is within its unchanged limit. The report lists every deleted case with what it pinned and where that behaviour is still covered, or that it no longer exists.

### M2 — One launch outcome: `mawsd`, or the repair window

**At its end.** The app's hosts are always `mawsd`'s three relay links. Main never opens the machine's data, never takes `spine.lock`, never forks an engine host or a pty host. A launch whose daemon cannot start opens the repair window (acceptance 2). `supervisor.shape` no longer selects anything, Settings has no shape switch, and `MAWS_HOST_MODE` is read by nothing in the app. `pnpm dev:scratch` runs a development build on a profile of its own.

**Touches.** `src/main/app.ts`; `src/main/hosts/` (`mode.ts`, `index.ts`, `daemon.ts`, `status.ts`); `src/main/ipc/router.ts`; `src/main/client/settings-store.ts`; `src/main/lifecycle/` (`engine-quit.ts`, `quit-guard.ts`); `src/main/connectors/index.ts`; `src/services/machine/identity.ts` and the home of `COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS`, for what main imports of them; new `src/main/repair/`, `src/preload/repair.ts`, `src/renderer/repair.html`, `src/renderer/src/repair/`; `electron.vite.config.ts`; `src/shared/settings/defaults.ts`; `src/renderer/src/settings-tab/` (`SupervisorSettings.tsx`, `copy.ts`) and the status row's dead branches; `package.json`; `test/services/boundary.test.ts` and a bundle check beside `src/engine-host/bundle-guard.test.ts`; the unit tests of all of these; `e2e/settings.spec.ts`; new `e2e/repair.spec.ts`; the comments that describe two roots (the inventory's §4.4 lists them).

**Interfaces.** §A (the launch outcome), §B (the repair window), §C (the e2e fault), below.

**Decisions.**

- *The launch has two outcomes and no third* (§A's table). The rows that returned `detached` with a reason now return that reason as a failure. On a failure main does not call `startMain`: it creates no hosts, installs no router and opens no store of the machine's. The profile's machine identity (`<userData>/machine.json`, OX2) is still read or minted first, as today, since OX2 gives that to whichever of the app and `mawsd` runs first.
- *What the window does not cover.* It is the main profile's launch outcome and nothing else. A profile that is not the main one answers `private` at once and starts nothing, as today: its daemon is started by the app's transports inside `startMain`, and making that start a condition of the launch would put a start and a welcome in front of every scratch launch the suites make, for a failure that only a broken build or a held lock produces and that the status row already reports. A daemon that fails after the launch, on any profile, is the transports' retry and the same status row, unchanged.
- *A development build on the main profile.* It uses the main profile's unit when one is installed and loaded, as today, and never installs one. With none it fails with `no-unit` and the window names `pnpm dev:scratch`, a new script that runs `electron-vite dev` with `MAWS_USER_DATA` defaulting to `~/Library/Application Support/MAWS-dev`, beside `dev:stub`'s and `dev:harness`'s profiles; a profile that is not the main one gets a private `mawsd` as always. The alternative, a private `mawsd` on the main profile when no unit exists, was not taken: the main profile's services would then be the unit's on one day and a private daemon's on another, and a unit the owner turned off in Login Items would be bypassed by the next development launch. The rule that results is one sentence: the main profile's machine runs under its unit or not at all.
- *The repair window is a page of its own* (§B): its own renderer entry and preload, built in every build, with the app's tokens. The alternative, a native alert, was not taken: the suites cannot drive one without a seam that skips it, it cannot show log lines a person can select, and it cannot change in place while an attempt runs. The page imports nothing of the app's stores or IPC: it must load when nothing else can.
- *Retrying.* One attempt runs at a time; it re-reads the unit's state and runs §A's table from the top. Besides the button, one attempt starts when the window regains focus after Open Login Items was used, and when a second launch of MAWS arrives or MAWS is opened again while it runs (macOS's `activate`, which starts no second process; Decision Log 16). No timer retries: a kickstart every few seconds against a job the owner turned off is noise in their system log. On success the window closes and the same process continues into `startMain`.
- *Settings.* No version bump (constraint 3). `supervisor.shape` and `supervisor.keepRunningDefault` are retired fields: both stay in the schema with their present types, so every existing file parses. `shape` reads and is stored as `daemon` whatever the file or a caller says, by one normalising function used at the client store's read and at its set; `keepRunningDefault` is kept as stored and read by nothing. Settings loses the shape switch and the "When quitting with work in flight" control with the copy only they used. `readLaunchShape` goes: the launch no longer depends on the settings file.
- *`MAWS_HOST_MODE` is retired.* Nothing in the app reads it to choose anything. Set to a value other than `daemon`, main logs once: `[hosts] MAWS_HOST_MODE is retired and ignored: the app runs on mawsd`. The scripts stop setting it. The helpers that strip it from a child's environment keep stripping it, since a peer build in cross-build still reads it.
- *Quitting.* The app's quit never stops, and never asks about, the machine's sessions or terminals; the unsaved-files question stays. The embedded keep-or-stop policy goes from `engine-quit.ts` and `quit-guard.ts`. The guard's behaviour on `mawsd` is not re-tuned here: tracker row #608's flake is not this milestone's.
- *What main keeps of the machine, on purpose* (constraint 5): the artifact scheme and render composition in `src/main/artifacts/`, and the two older-daemon answers. In `src/main/artifacts/index.ts` only the configuration that existed for a launch with no daemon to ask goes, and only if it separates cleanly from the older-daemon path; otherwise it stays and the report says so.
- *The absence checks.* `test/services/boundary.test.ts` reverses its allowance: no module under `src/main/`, `src/preload/` or `src/renderer/` imports `src/services/spine-root.ts`, `src/services/entry.ts` or `src/services/checkouts-root.ts`, directly or through a re-export. Main takes `COMMAND_BUDGETS_MS` and the identity bootstrap from modules that import none of the three; the command registration that `startMachineIdentity` did for the embedded shape goes with it. A bundle check reads the built `out/main/index.js` and `out/main/app.js` with their chunks and fails if any of the three modules is in their graph. If, after the move, the app's graph holds no module under `src/services/` at all, assert that wider rule instead.
- *`host.status`* reports `daemon` as its transport and no unavailability on every launch that reaches the app. The renderer's branches and copy for the other values go. A schema member is removed only if no process of another build sends or reads it (constraint 4).
- *Where there is no unit manager* (the app run on a platform that is not a Mac), the main profile's outcome is `no-unit`.

**Does not touch.** `src/services/**`, `src/mawsd/**` and the engine host beyond the import move above; `src/shared/host/detached-transport.ts`; the older-daemon answers; the build check that asks a daemon of another build to restart; the index; the client settings version; anything of the project registry.

**Proves.** Acceptance 1, 2 and 3. Unit: every row of §A's table; the normalisation at read and at set, with every other setting kept; one attempt at a time, the focus retry and the second-launch retry; the log tail's cut. E2e (`e2e/repair.spec.ts`, added to the `e2e:supervisor-daemon` script's file list so that no gate list changes, and skipping without that script's `MAWS_SUPERVISOR_E2E=1` as `supervisor.spec.ts` does; `e2e-default-specs.test.mjs`'s count moves again): cases 1 to 4 under §C. `e2e/settings.spec.ts`'s shape-flip case becomes §C's case 5. Acceptance 3's data is the cross-build scene §C names. The unit's own path end to end, a LaunchAgent under a scratch label, stays the `daemon-unit` group's. `--gates all` and `--gates packaged` are green on the branch's head.

### M2b — Resume after a wake by design, main's log on disk, and what the readiness read found

**At its end.** A wake is noticed with the sleep's length. After a sleep of 10 s or more the app drops every socket it held before the sleep, and for the next 60 s it learns that a path is reachable from bare TCP connects, and each carrier makes its own attempt when its path answers. In the common case §F states, each open subscription of a link is then answered within 1 s of its carrier's path being reachable, on a network inside §F's round-trip times and with §F's term `J` as far as the gate shows it. The org feed and each project socket take the same drop, reopen with one open in flight, and are checked for life by a protocol ping at all times. Main writes every log line to `<profile>/logs/main.log` with its time, and the lines M4's 7e reads exist there and in `mawsd.log`. A peer message in the session pane shows its sender's owners under P5 Decision Log 111's rule. `host.log` says when a launch's ring first evicted and when a read was served from the journal. `artifact_export`'s description no longer says it needs the app. A failed hub connection of `mawsd` names its error code and addresses.

**Touches.** New `src/main/net/` (`wake.ts`, `reach.ts`) and `src/main/log-file.ts`; `src/main/app.ts` (the log's capture and its install under the profile lock; one line at a second launch's hand-off; the wake source and the reach made once and handed to the feed, the machines and the org session; its two `powerMonitor` lines go); `src/main/machines/index.ts` and `src/main/machines/remote/` (`link.ts`, `carriers.ts`, `endpoint-records.ts`, `subs.ts`, `acts.ts`, `source.ts`, `timing.ts`); `src/main/org/` (`registry-feed.ts`, `timing.ts`, `org-session.ts`, `channel/channel.ts`, `channel/project-socket.ts`); `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts` and `src/endpoint/hub-link.ts`; `src/engine-host/session-stream.ts`; new `src/shared/host/error-words.ts`; `src/shared/engine/peer-envelope.ts`; `src/shared/maws/tools.ts` with `spikes/protocol-probe/scenarios/pass3/p4-maws-tools.json`; `src/renderer/src/sidebar/owners.ts` and `src/renderer/src/transcript/` (`model/rows.ts`, `model/derive.ts`, `view/rows/AgentMessage.tsx`); `e2e/support/remote-scene.ts`, `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` (one assertion), new `test/support/main-log.ts` and `test/support/wake-probe.mjs`; the unit tests of all of these. Nothing under `.github/` or `scripts/`, and not `package.json`: M1 is editing them, and M2b adds no script, no spec file and no gate group. Nothing under `apps/org-protocol`: catch-up and `connectChannel` are as they are. If Task 8 is ruled in, §L's files under `src/engine-host/` as well.

Two test files that M1 also edits are M2b's too: `src/main/lifecycle.test.ts` and `src/main/sessions/main-wiring.test.ts`, which load the real startup root with `electron` mocked. In each, the mocked `powerMonitor` has only `on` and gains `off`, which the wake source calls at shutdown; and each mocks the root's `log-file` module, so that the capture and the install do nothing there and no wrapped console outlives a test (`log-file.test.ts` covers the module itself). M1's edits to both files are on branch `p5-o15` (27740743a and its merge of master), where the `powerMonitor` line reads as it does on master, so the edit fits either version. M2b's branch merges `origin/master` once M1 and M2 have landed; if its task reaches these files first, it keeps its edit to those lines and resolves at the merge. Class 1a green on the merged head is the proof.

**Interfaces.** §D to §L, below; §L only if Task 8 is ruled in. §A's refused-start rule and §B's words for it are M2's to build; Decision Log 6 records them with M2b because the same read found them.

**Decisions.**

- *A wake is noticed with its length* (§D). One module, `src/main/net/wake.ts`, listens to `powerMonitor`'s `suspend`, `resume` and `unlock-screen`, and nothing else in main does: the remote source, the org feed and the project sockets take its notices. A `resume` reports the time since the `suspend`; for a wake that sent no `resume`, a 1 s timer reports a tick that came 10 s or more late as a wake of that length. Electron's documentation says of `resume` only "Emitted when system is resuming" and names no kind of wake, so nothing here depends on which wakes send it. A dark wake, in which macOS runs for a short time with the display off, is a wake like any other: the notice cannot tell them apart, the sockets are dropped and the paths probed, and if the network is up the links come back until the Mac sleeps again; its cost is one reconnect. The timer cannot tell a sleep from a main process that did not run for 10 s. That is reported as a wake too, rightly, since the peers saw the same silence, and at most once a minute, so a main process that keeps stalling does not reconnect in a loop. `unlock-screen`, a window's focus and the address-change poll keep the probes they make today. The timer alone was not taken: it would add up to a second to every wake that does send `resume`.
- *A sleep of 10 s or more drops every socket from before it, at once.* Each link's channel, its relay socket and any attempt in flight; the org feed's socket; each project socket. None is pinged first. A connection that is only read from, or whose far end dropped it during the sleep, reports nothing until a write fails; a ping's 2 s deadline is time the bound does not have; and resuming from a cursor is the designed path, so dropping a channel that happened to be alive costs one reconnect and the reopening of the byte streams on it (a remote terminal, a forwarded port), which end with their channel at any loss. The threshold is the endpoint's own limit. It closes a channel at the third ping period in a row in which it heard nothing, 15 to 28 s after the last message it heard, and that is as little as 10 s after the sleep begins when the sleep starts with one of its pings unanswered (`PING_MS` and `#arm` in `src/endpoint/channels.ts`: two more periods of at least 5 s). A second channel from the same device does not replace the first there: channels are keyed by their carrier, so the endpoint keeps both until the old one's own silence closes it. The hub closes no silent app socket by a timer (`apps/desk-hub/src/objects/relay.ts`: "The ends drive the heartbeats; this object keeps no timer"), so it gives no later threshold, and whether one of its sockets outlived the sleep is the network's to know, not the app's. A test holds `WAKE_DROP_MS` to twice the endpoint's shortest period. The alternative, ping first and keep what answers, is today's probe: it is right for a focus, an unlock and a sleep under 10 s, and stays there.
- *For 60 s after a wake or an address change, a probe that costs nothing finds the path, and each carrier tries on its own* (§E, §F). In this window the direct carrier and the relay are two lanes of a link, each with its own targets, its own probing and its own attempt. A lane's targets, each tailnet address of the cached endpoint record for one and the hub for the other, are probed with bare TCP connects, one every 100 ms. The first that connects is closed and is the signal that the path is reachable, and the lane makes its attempt at once, whatever the other lane is doing. The first lane to complete a handshake is adopted. One prober serves the hub for every link's relay lane, the org feed and the project sockets. When the window closes, today's backoff takes over. A probe and not the open itself, for two costs. The direct listener takes a token from its per-source bucket (a burst of 3, then 5 a minute; `HANDSHAKE_LIMITS`, which the app's `DIRECT_BUCKET` mirrors) at every upgrade request, before any key work (`#upgrade` in `src/endpoint/direct-listener.ts`), so repeated opens would empty it in under a second and the open meant for the handshake would be answered 503. And every open that reaches the hub costs it an Access check and a registry read, and on the feed a whole snapshot. A TCP connect that closes costs neither, because it never becomes an upgrade. The probe's socket is closed, not handed to the WebSocket client: the client takes a ready socket only through its HTTP request's options, the hub's would need TLS started on it, and the dial inside the window would then differ from the dial outside it; the round trip this costs is in §F's budget. Two lanes that race, and not one attempt at a time, because a path whose TCP connects can still hang above TCP, at the hub's TLS or upgrade or at the listener's: with one attempt at a time the hung one holds the working carrier behind it for as long as its deadlines last. The endpoint keeps a device's channels by carrier, so two handshakes in flight from one device are legal, and the cost is at most one token and one relay socket a try. For the same reason a lane's attempt has the window's deadlines, 2 s for the relay socket's open and for each handshake message where today's are 10 s, so that a hung attempt frees its lane; a lane whose attempt fails backs off, 100 ms at first and growing to 2 s, and goes on probing meanwhile; and a lane makes at most six attempts each time the link enters *lanes*, the direct lane dialling only while the bucket would keep a token after it. When direct wins, the relay's attempt is aborted. When the relay wins, the direct lane goes on, and its channel, when it completes, takes the relay's place by today's switch: a swap in the first seconds after a wake ends fewer byte streams than the 60 s recheck would end a minute into use, and aborting a direct attempt the bucket was already charged for, only to dial it again, would charge it twice. An attempt in flight when the window opens is aborted: it began before the path returned and would hold the link to its deadlines, about 23 s today (the record fetch, the direct race, the relay open, the handshake). The lanes use the cached endpoint record at once while the hub's is read in the background, and never a relay socket from before the window; and in the window or out of it the cached relay socket is closed when its channel is lost for `timeout`, since reusing a dead one cost 10 s a handshake. *The floor* keeps the window from ever leaving a link worse off than today's code. A probe is given 3 s, so that a slow or lossy path still answers one; the thirty in flight for a target are thirty sockets that carry nothing. And when a link has been 10 s in *lanes* without a channel, or sooner once both lanes are spent, the lanes start no more attempts and today's loop runs alone, whole and under today's deadlines: the record read from the hub, the race over the direct addresses, the relay. A lane attempt in flight at that moment is not cut: it runs to its end first, since a path that first answers in the tenth second would otherwise lose the attempt the bound rests on. The lanes and today's loop are never concurrent on a link: it is in one of three modes (§F), so there is one direct dialler at a time, as today, and nothing to arbitrate between a lane and today's attempt. The floor does not end a lane whose path has not come back yet: a lane that has had no answer since the window opened goes on waiting, from the floor's time on, and its first answer returns the link to *lanes*, aborting today's attempt if one is running, once for each lane. The window is 60 s because a network can take longer than 10 s to return, and today's attempt cannot make the bound over the relay when the direct path is silent, since it gives the direct race its second before it opens the relay. So a link is without today's loop for 10 s and at most one attempt's deadlines more, and a path that returns at any time in the window is met by its lane. Five alternatives lost. A floor that ended the lanes for the rest of the window was this document's fifth text: it served only a network that returned within 10 s of the wake, and it cut an attempt begun in the tenth second (Decision Log 10). Today's attempt running beside the lanes after the floor was this document's third text: two diallers then drew on one bucket that reserves nothing, and a relay adopted by one of them while the other was still in its handshake had no rule (Decision Log 9). One attempt at a time with the other target remembered was this document's first text and the review's first finding (Decision Log 8). Shorter timeouts on sequential attempts cannot give the bound: an attempt begun just before the path returns holds the link until its deadline. Staggered WebSocket opens, with the handshake on the first to connect, was the architect's first decision and was withdrawn at the desk track's read of the listener and the hub, for the two costs above (Decision Log 6).
- *The 1 s is shown by arithmetic, for a wake that meets six conditions* (§F, "The budget, and the wake it is claimed for"; the same list stands in acceptance 6 and in M4's 7e row). No catch-up page: when the two ends hold different org heads, as after a machine key or the org state changed during the sleep, the handshake takes one more dial for each page; that recovery stays exactly as it is. At most six subscriptions on the link, two open sessions with their feeds. Round trips and `J` inside the arithmetic. On the lane that is adopted, the first attempt after its first probe answer succeeds: a failed attempt costs its own time and a backoff under any design, so the lane's backoff starts at 100 ms. Where that lane is direct, two tokens in the bucket when the lane made its attempt, which any sleep of 24 s leaves there. And at most 4 KiB written on the new channel ahead of the `sub`s: `adopt` first resends the acts still inside their 60 s and asks about the older ones, an act may be as large as the channel's 4 MiB and the queries are as many as the acts still open, and the transfer of either is bounded by nothing here; a query is a few hundred bytes, so the hour asleep meets this unless more than about a dozen acts are open. None of the six is about when the path returns: at any time while the window is open, a lane's first answer starts its attempt at once. A wake outside the conditions is neither a pass nor a fail of the bound: its lines show which condition it missed, and M4's row says how it is judged. Inside them the direct carrier fits while six tailnet round trips and `J` come to at most 800 ms, and the relay while seven round trips to the hub, three from the hub to the machine, and `J` come to at most 500 ms: the hub's own work on an upgrade was measured on staging at 220 to 240 ms and is allowed 300 ms, the largest single share of the relay's second (Decision Log 6). `J` is the endpoint's work between a `sub` and its `subbed`. For a cursor the engine host's ring still holds it is a few reads from memory. For a cursor the ring has evicted, which is the case after an hour asleep with the session working, it is a read of the launch's journal from its first row: measured at about 35 ms for each 10,000 rows before the cursor, so it is not bounded by design today and grows with the launch's history. §F gives the journal size up to which each carrier still fits: about 160,000 rows on the direct carrier at a 40 ms round trip, about 57,000 on the relay at 30 ms. The design that bounds it is §L's checkpoints: Task 8, built in M2b if the owner rules so (Decision Log 8), otherwise a board ticket. A lane A stream's `J` is bounded, since its endpoint answers with no read; after an hour its answer is always a snapshot, whose content is sminos's to send (§F). Outside the window the bound is not promised: a path that returns more than 60 s after the wake is found by today's backoff, or by the next address change, which opens a window of its own within the poll's 2 s.
- *The org feed and the project socket* (§G) take the wake drop and reopen with one open in flight, the first at the hub prober's first answer. Never overlapping opens: each feed open makes the hub send the grid, the state version and every listings page, and each project open its welcome, machines, presence, pending, ops and deliveries, so an open closed unused costs a whole snapshot. Their liveness check, on both and at all times, is a WebSocket protocol ping with a deadline for its pong. Never an application message: the Registry drops one, so the deadline would always fire, and the project socket refuses it and writes an audit row for each. The hub's runtime answers a protocol ping itself without waking the object (Cloudflare's Durable Objects documentation, "Use WebSockets", "Automatic ping/pong handling"; measured on staging, Decision Log 6). When the app drops one of these sockets it terminates it and counts it ended in the same turn. It never waits for the hub's close frame: neither object completes a close handshake, and a client `close(1000)` ends as 1006 about 10 s later. Today both already open the next socket without waiting, since each forgets the old socket before it closes it; what changes is that the old one is terminated, not left half closed for those 10 s. The 1 s bound is acceptance 7's for subscriptions over links and is not claimed for these two sockets. §G states their target, 2 s to the feed's last listings page. The gate case holds the feed to it on loopback: without a bound, the defect this milestone removes, a feed that stays dead after a wake, could return with the gate green. 7e records it live. Both sockets have the links' floor: one that has waited 10 s on the hub's prober opens anyway.
- *What "resumes from its cursor" means.* A session's stream resubscribes with the cursor of the last event it delivered, and the endpoint answers `subbed` with `replay` when it can go on from there. A feed (attention, artifacts, a checkout, the browser inventory, a project's channel feed) resubscribes with no cursor and is answered with a snapshot: that is RL3's design for a feed, not a loss (`SubscribeRequest` in `src/main/machines/remote/subs.ts`). Acceptance 7 cites RL3, so both are resumed when the endpoint's `subbed` arrives, and that arrival is what the 1 s is measured to. A session stream answered `snapshot` or `missing`, because the launch changed during the sleep or the journal no longer holds the range, is resumed as RL3 defines it, and the record says which answer each got. The org feed has no cursor and reads its listings whole; the project socket resumes after its offset. M4's 7e row is worded this way.
- *Main's log is a file, and a private one* (§H): `<profile>/logs/main.log`, every line with its time, 8 MiB and one previous generation, written beside stdout and stderr, which keep every line they have today. Under the profile, not Electron's logs directory, which the profiles of one build share: a scratch launch must not write into the owner's log, and the gate case reads its own launch's file. Only the instance that holds the profile lock writes it. What a launch says before it holds the lock is kept in memory and written then with its own times, so the file has the launch's first lines and a second launch can never rotate the first's file away. Opening it can never stop a launch: a directory that cannot be made or tightened turns the file log off with one line on stderr. The file is a diagnostic on the owner's disk and holds whatever main's console holds, file paths and error texts included. What M2b promises to be free of session content is the lines §H lists, and only those leave a machine as evidence, picked by §K's patterns. M2b changes where lines go and adds §H's lines; it changes no existing line and adds one at a second launch's hand-off. This closes tracker row #586 (the main process's log). The alternative, timestamps on stdout alone, leaves a Dock-launched app with no log, which is the row.
- *The proof resembles a wake* (§I). The existing forced drop destroys every socket and calls the probe at the instant of the restore, which is why it measures tens of ms. The new case leaves the old sockets open and silent, injects the wake notice through an e2e-only seam, restores the path at a time the app is not told, and reads the app's own log for each `subbed`. It runs with six subscriptions on the link, with two streams whose cursors the ring has really evicted, and, on the relay's launch, with the direct path answering TCP and hanging above it. It is a second test of `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`: a 1 s bound belongs in the measured group, which runs alone on the mini, and `e2e:remote` holds no wall-clock bound by its own rule; a second test of an existing file changes no script, no gate list and no count that M1 is editing. Under `e2e:remote-latency-check` it runs unbounded, as the file's first test does. It reads the log file because that is the instrument 7e uses on the day: the gate then proves the lines as well as the behaviour. The link's state table is proved below it, on a fake clock with fake carriers and a fake prober, in `src/main/machines/remote/link.test.ts`.
- *The owner on a peer message* (§J). sminos puts the sender session's owners into its frame line, after the sender's alias; MAWS cuts that line from the body it draws and so drew no owner. The label is read from the frame in the renderer, from the `user.message` that carried the envelope, and no field is added to the `peer.message` event: that event crosses builds, a field on it is a wire addition and a minor (constraint 4), and the text is already in the renderer's hands. The rule is the sidebar's own function. What MAWS draws is a display of sminos's frame, not a verification of its own, and §J says what that is worth today. The alternative, an `owners` field on the event, costs a minor for a label.
- *Three small fixes.* (1) `src/engine-host/session-stream.ts` logs, through the log it already takes, the first eviction of a launch's ring and each read the journal serves that does not continue the page before it (§H's lines), so 2c's record can say where a replay came from. (2) `artifact_export`'s description ends "Needs the MAWS app open." (`MAWS_TOOLS` in `src/shared/maws/tools.ts`). It does not: the export bridge asks any client that renders, and `mawsd`'s render service is one with no app (`answersRenders` in `src/engine-host/attachment.ts`), which is P5 acceptance 10. The sentence becomes "Works whether or not the MAWS app is open." What pins the descriptions is `src/shared/maws/search-tools.test.ts`, which holds `spikes/protocol-probe/scenarios/pass3/p4-maws-tools.json` equal to the source less the withdrawn `message` tool: the same sentence is changed by hand in that file's `artifact_export` entry. The file is not regenerated with `MAWS_WRITE_PROBE_TOOLS=1`, which would drop `message` and fail the test's first assertion; the recorded fixtures under `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/p4/` keep the old sentence, as what that run was served, and no probe test compares it. (3) `HubLink` in `src/endpoint/hub-link.ts` keeps only an error's `message`, which is empty for a connect that failed on several addresses. It keeps §H's error words instead, so the next swap's log names the cause of the 71 s the MacBook's `mawsd` took to reach the hub (P5 Decision Log 262).
- *Out of M2b, recorded as tracker rows by M5.* `mawsd`'s own hub link after a wake: it notices within one 15 s heartbeat and then follows its backoff; it is how other machines reach this one, not a subscription of the app's, and acceptance 7's sentence does not cover it. `remote.gap`, a `missing` answer told to the renderer, is still not drawn (tracker row #407, the renderer shows no gap on a resume that lost a range). `raceDirect`'s losers that opened after the race settled are charged by the listener and not by the app's bucket: not fixed here although M2b edits `carriers.ts`, because the listener keys its bucket by source address and a device's IPv4 and IPv6 tailnet addresses are two sources, so charging the app's one bucket for every opened socket would make it stricter than the listener; the right mirror is a bucket per address family, which is a change to the bucket's model. The lanes do not use the race; today's attempt does, inside the window past the floor as outside it. The project socket's wake path has no case in the built app, for §I's reason. sminos's frame lets an alias imitate the owner label until sminos writes such an alias as a string literal (§J); the fix is the desk track's. The hub spends about 190 ms of every request on its Access check and its registry round trip, which is most of the relay carrier's share of the wake budget; shortening it is the desk track's. On a new channel the subscriptions are written after everything the act table sends (`adopt`'s order), so an act of up to 4 MiB still inside its 60 s, or queries for many open acts, delay every `subbed` by their transfer: M2b names that as the bound's sixth condition and logs it, and does not change the order, which is the link's behaviour outside any window; whether the `sub`s can go first, without a subscription's answer reading the machine before an act the person already made has reached it, is for the row to settle, with the endpoint's order of handling read first.

**Tasks, in build order.** Each is one commit or a few, with its unit tests; class 1a runs green on the head after each task that touches TypeScript (constraint 6).

1. *The log file and the error words.* `src/main/log-file.ts`; in `src/main/app.ts` its capture at load, its install under the profile lock and the second launch's line; the `log-file` mock in the two startup tests; `src/shared/host/error-words.ts`; `src/endpoint/hub-link.ts`. Proof: §H's unit behaviours; the hub link's failed line carries the code and the inner addresses; `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` finds the file after a launch, in the packaged job too.
2. *The three small fixes and the owner label.* `src/engine-host/session-stream.ts`; `src/shared/maws/tools.ts` with the probe's snapshot; `src/shared/engine/peer-envelope.ts`, `src/renderer/src/sidebar/owners.ts`, the transcript's model and row. Proof: the two `host.log` lines, each once where §H says; `search-tools.test.ts` green on the edited snapshot; §J's cases.
3. *The wake source and the reach.* `src/main/net/wake.ts`, `src/main/net/reach.ts`, with the e2e seam. Nothing uses them yet. Proof: §D's and §E's unit behaviours.
4. *The link.* `carriers.ts` (both opens take an abort signal), `endpoint-records.ts` (`cached`), `timing.ts` (the window's deadlines, the lane backoff and count), `link.ts` (§F: the three modes, the lanes, the floor, the abort through every dial), `subs.ts` (§H's `subbed` line), `acts.ts` (what its `onCurrent` returns, §F's sixth condition), `source.ts` and `src/main/machines/index.ts` (the notices), `src/main/app.ts` (the wiring, the dev session's and the relay-only bench's direct target), and `off` on the two startup tests' `powerMonitor`. Proof: §F's table, row by row, in `link.test.ts`; every existing case of `link.test.ts`, `source.test.ts` and `subs.test.ts` unchanged where no window opens.
5. *The org feed and the project socket.* `registry-feed.ts`, `channel/project-socket.ts`, `channel/channel.ts`, `org-session.ts`, `timing.ts`, and `src/main/app.ts` (the wake source and the reach handed to the feed and the org session; the two `powerMonitor` lines removed). Proof: §G's unit behaviours.
6. *The machine's line.* `src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts`. Proof: one line per `subbed` sent, in §H's words, in the table's unit test.
7. *The gate case.* `e2e/support/remote-scene.ts` (the pipe's `stall`, `hold` and `requests`), `test/support/main-log.ts`, the second test of `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`. Proof: §I, on the mini.
8. *Checkpoints in the session stream, only if the owner rules so* (§L; Decision Log 8). `src/engine-host/session-stream.ts`, `session.ts`, `journal.ts`, `ipc.ts`. Proof: §L's property test and its count of rows read. Without the ruling it is not built, and §L is the body of a board ticket.
9. *The 7e probe.* `test/support/wake-probe.mjs` and its test. Proof: §K's unit behaviours.

**Does not touch.** The wire: no command, event, capability or schema member changes, and `src/shared/ipc/wire-manifest.json` is as it was; a builder who finds a wire change is needed stops and reports it. `apps/org-protocol`: the handshake, its catch-up and its redials are as they are. The engine host's journal read and ring, unless Task 8 is ruled in: `J` is named, measured and gated at the gate's size (§F), and §L's checkpoints are built only on the owner's ruling. `src/services/**` and `src/mawsd/**`. The endpoint and the engine host beyond the log lines named above. The link's behaviour while no window is open, but for the relay socket closed at a `timeout` loss. The recheck's 60 s, the backoff tables, the silence pings, the bucket. The existing forced-drop case and every bound of `e2e:remote-latency` (constraint 2: if the changed link moves one, the work stops with the numbers). `desk-hub`. The repair window, §A to §C, and every file M1 and M2 touch except the three "Touches" names: `src/main/app.ts`, where M2b's edits sit at the log's capture and install and where the feed and the machines are composed, and the two startup tests, `src/main/lifecycle.test.ts` and `src/main/sessions/main-wiring.test.ts`, where they are the two lines "Touches" gives. Whichever branch lands second merges master and keeps both sides' edits in those three.

**Proves.** Acceptance 6. Unit: every behaviour §D to §H, §J and §K list, and §L's proof if Task 8 is built. E2e: §I's case in the measured group, with the link's 1 s and the feed's 2 s held; `e2e:remote` green as it stands; `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s log assertion. `--gates all` and `--gates packaged` are green on the branch's head, the second because main's file log is new behaviour in a packaged app (the profile's path, a launch with no terminal), and `e2e:remote-latency` is green on the mini with its first test's bounds unmoved.

### M3 — The final build on the three machines

**At its end.** The MacBook, the mini and `dc-linux171-1e68` run one master commit, **F**, that holds M1, M2 and M2b; production's hub is deployed from a master that holds PR #538; staging runs F. Each machine keeps cf30dafdd as its rollback.

**Decisions.**

- *F is fixed when the first machine swaps.* A docs-only landing after that does not move it. A code landing after that reopens M4 for the claims it can reach, by the architect's reading of the diff, recorded in the Decision Log.
- *Gates on F before the swap*, each through `scripts/ci-gate.sh`: `--gates all` and `--gates packaged` (acceptance 1b); the measured groups `e2e:local-latency-daemon` (1c), `e2e:daemon-idle`, `e2e:daemon-idle-render`, `e2e:daemon-idle-session-host` and `e2e:daemon-idle-enrolled` (14, the mini's series), `e2e:remote-latency` (4m to 4q, the one-host leg, and M2b's wake case, O15's acceptance 6); `e2e:cross-build` against the newest master commit one wire minor below F, 63443a3bd while F is at 3.33 (13: the gate script runs the two-build check only across a minor, and one minor is what the item names). A job that is green only on a rerun is recorded with its failure's signature. A job red twice blocks the swap.
- *The hub deploy is the owner's command*, from a checkout of F (`production-run.sh checkout`, then the deploy command it prints). The desk track deploys staging.
- *The swap is the live check of two claims*, so two things are staged first. For 1d, on each Mac, a read of the profile's counts (projects, sessions, artifacts, library items, drafts, open tabs in the layout) and of the index file's identity. For 13, on the mini, a lane B session mid-turn with a background shell running and a permission pending. After the swap the counts are read again, the index is the same file with no rebuild line in `mawsd.log`, and the staged turn completes with its input applied once, its permission card shown once and the shell's completion reported once.
- *The swap needs the owner's approval* at the time. The desk track swaps the MacBook and the VM, mini-ops the mini, as at every swap. Each reports its times, builds, wire, sminos version, the count of error lines, and how long the hub took to connect.

**Proves.** Acceptance 1b, 1c, 1d, 13, and the mini's half of 14, on F.

### M4 — The closing run

**At its end.** Every claim has its result on F. The table below is the run. Its claim ids are the evidence inventory's, which also holds each claim's earlier evidence.

**How a claim is checked.**

- **G**, a gate or a measured group on F's tree (M3 ran them).
- **S**, the swap itself, staged before and read after (M3).
- **L**, live on the production machines on F, driven by an agent: the MacBook's MAWS through `cua_repl` with the owner's approval and the window given for the time, the mini's through mini-ops, the VM through the desk track. mini-ops acts on the mini's MAWS through `machine.sock` (`session.create`, `session.send`, `session.answer`, `session.events`) and on its terminal sessions through tmux. The mini's window, in the rows that name it (2b, 8a, 8b), is read and driven by the architect through the enrolled `cua` device `mini`, whose two macOS permissions the owner had already granted (a read on 2026-11-10 returned the window's tree and a screenshot with no prompt); mini-ops keeps the readings from the mini's service beside it. Every Touch ID is the owner's.
- **T**, the desk track's run on staging on F, for the cases the item names staging for or that production cannot host.
- **B**, the real-network bench from the MacBook.
- **O**, the owner's own hands.
- **C**, cited from the record, with the reason a new check adds nothing.

A claim that fails is not retried until it passes. It is recorded as failed with what was seen, and then either fixed (a code landing, which moves F by M3's rule) or put to the owner as a ruling: waive, or carry with a tracker row. Nothing is waived by the architect.

| Claim | How | What is done on F | Passes when |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1a signed-out parity | L | F's packaged app on a scratch profile on the MacBook, signed out, with a private `mawsd`: open a project, a new chat with one real turn and one permission, Files open, edit and save, Source Control's diff, a terminal, an artifact made and opened, the library, a browser tab, a setting changed and kept across a relaunch, a quit with the turn still running | each surface works as at c3507722d, with nothing asking to sign in |
| 1b e2e groups on `mawsd` | G | `--gates all`, `--gates packaged` | green; rerun passes named |
| 1c OX7 | G | `e2e:local-latency-daemon` on the mini | p50 ≤ 26.45 ms, p95 ≤ 42.56 ms |
| 1d everything present, no re-index | S | counts and the index file's identity (its inode and birth time) before and after the swap, on both Macs; Customize › Connectors read on each Mac for O4's adoption record | every count equal or higher by what was made since; same index file; no rebuild line; the connectors' grants listed as connected, with no new sign-in |
| 2a tool calls with the app quit | L | on the MacBook, a lane B session told to wait a minute, then write an artifact and refresh a dataset with its `maws` tools; MAWS quit during the wait for three minutes. Before the quit: the dataset is one with a live query, its connector tool is granted ("Allow … to read" answered, so no request waits for a window), and `mawsd` is the process that declares the connectors | both calls completed inside the quit window, by their times; neither tool cell reads "MAWS is not open; the dataset cannot be refreshed" |
| 2b index and attention keep deriving | L | in the same run: the session's state read on the mini while the MacBook's MAWS is quit, by `state-stamp` run there (a reading about every 355 ms, each change given as the window between two readings), and in the mini's MAWS window, read by the architect through the `cua` device; a terminal session's transcript written during the quit | the mini reads Working, then the next state, with no pause; the transcript is listed at reopening |
| 2c no gap at reopening | L | the same session reopened; the engine host's `host.log` read for M2b's two lines (§H) | the transcript is whole, the card shows once; the record says, by those lines, whether the replay came from the ring or the journal, and if the ring never evicted that half stays on O3-D's gate |
| 3a one sidebar, by org project, with machines | L | the MacBook's sidebar read with sessions live on all three machines | sessions of the mini and the VM sit under the org projects this Mac holds, each row with its machine. Projects this Mac does not hold group only with the registry (Decision Log 265), carried |
| 3b states match `sminos list --org` within 2 s | L | five state changes of one mini session that is not private and has not been opened in the MacBook's MAWS since the app started (a session opened earlier leaves its record in the window after its tab closes, so a closed tab is not this case), each timed twice on the MacBook's clock: at the row, by a `cua_repl` loop that reads the row glyph's `aria-label` (Working, Needs you, Failed, Done, Idle) and stamps each change; at `sminos list --org`, by `state-stamp`, which brackets each reading in ms. Then a second reading, on a lane B session of the mini whose tab is open in the MacBook's MAWS, the case PR #556 corrected: the changes of one turn with one permission (Working, Needs you, Working, Done), timed the same way | each pair within 2 s, in both readings |
| 3c no project, under the machine | L | on the mini, row 6's terminal session in `~/o15-scratch/6b-mini`, a folder in no org project (no session is started at the mini's home, where macOS's folder prompts would wait for the owner); on the VM, a session at the machine's home | each sits under its machine |
| 4a to 4l, twelve actions on each machine | L | from the MacBook, one fresh lane B session on the mini and one on the VM, in a scratch folder with no output style: stream, send, steer, interrupt, approve, deny, answer a question, send an image, show a diff with an untracked file in it, open and edit a file, run a terminal, and open, edit and screenshot an artifact | all 24 cells pass; for 4a the reply's text is seen to grow at three or more readings in one turn |
| 4m to 4p the remote bounds | G, B | `e2e:remote-latency` on the mini (M3); `e2e:remote-latency-live` from the MacBook in the quiet window against one bench machine, `vm-bench` on a devcloud VM, with the bench app on a fresh profile; the app is paired and the machine enrolled on production through the app's own sheets for the run, and both are removed after (O7's document, "The live bench"; tracker row #605). Both carriers, direct and relay, are measured against that machine, which is P5 acceptance 4's "each transport the app uses"; there is no bench machine on the mini, since it would add two Touch IDs and no further transport. The run's own precheck (the runner's 1-minute load under 5) passes before the owner is asked for anything | on each carrier, delta to paint p50 ≤ local p50 + one round trip + 10 ms; a command ≤ two round trips + 20 ms; open to first row ≤ 1.5 s; resume ≤ engine start + one round trip. A run its own precheck or clock check voids is void, never averaged in; the clock check is O7's as corrected by PR #555 (a carrier is void when the two ends' offset intervals cannot share one offset within 5 ms). A void or failed run is tried once more in the same window; a second is put to the owner |
| 4q each transport | B, L | the bench's direct and relay legs; the link's hover read during 4a to 4l; then the MacBook's direct path forced down for a minute (Tailscale off on the MacBook), the hover read again, and the path restored | both legs measured; the hover names the carrier in use: "direct (tailnet)", and "via the hub" while the direct path is down, the link back on the direct carrier within about a minute of its return, the recheck's period |
| 5a, 5b spawn in the sidebar within 5 s | L | "New chat" on the mini and on the VM from the MacBook. The row's first sight is timed on the MacBook by the same `cua_repl` loop; the engine's first event is the `at` field of the first row of the session's journal on the machine, and that field alone is read, since the row holds session content; the two clocks' offset is read before and after | ≤ 5 s on each |
| 6a, 6b lane A's controls | L | a terminal session on the MacBook and one on the mini, from the MacBook's MAWS: send, steer, interrupt, answer a question, approve, set the model, set the effort | each takes effect in the terminal session |
| 6c unreachable controls say why | L | the disabled controls of those two sessions read | each is disabled with its reason |
| 6d no bridge, read only | L | a terminal session started without sminos's bridge | its row opens read-only |
| 7a to 7d the laptop asleep | O | the MacBook asleep for an hour with MAWS open; before it, a session on the mini and one on the VM given an hour's work that asks a question at about minute 10 and meets a permission prompt at about minute 25 | both sessions worked through the hour by their logs; each card reached the phone and was answered there; each waited until its answer |
| 7e within 1 s of the network being reachable after wake, every subscription resumes from its cursor, with no loss and no duplicate (RL3) | O, L | MAWS launched as always: `<profile>/logs/main.log` (M2b) is the record. A Channel tab is left open, and a session of each machine open in the pane. Before the sleep §K's probe is started on the MacBook and left running: an overlapping TCP connect every 100 ms to the hub's host on 443 and to each machine's direct port, 47830, each try's start and end recorded; and after the wake, for 30 s on each machine, to the hub, for `m`. After the wake the probe's `read` gives, for each target, the first try begun after the wake that succeeded, with `d`, `h` and `m`; `extract` copies §K's lines, and nothing else, out of `main.log` and each machine's `mawsd.log` and `host.log` (as written, `host.log`'s stream lines carry `warn: [engine-host] ` and `mawsd.log`'s endpoint lines carry `[endpoint] ` before §K's text; `extract` accepts both). For `J`: a session that was idle through the hour has been released on its machine, which stops its engine and keeps its stream, its checkpoints and the last 500 events of its ring, so the first read after the wake is answered from the ring, `host.log` gains no `from the journal` line for it, and its `J` is the endpoint's `subbed … after <n> ms` line alone. A journal line appears only for a session that wrote more than 500 events past the held cursor during the hour; with §L's checkpoints built it ends `, from the checkpoint at seq <n>` where a checkpoint served the read, and its rows and ms are that stream's `J`. A journal line with no such suffix for a cursor past the first 10,000 rows means `mawsd` or its engine host restarted on that machine during the hour, since checkpoints are kept in memory only; that is recorded with the line. A `live checkpoints off for launch` line for a session's launch is copied beside its `J` | "resumes" is M2b's reading: a subscription is resumed when the endpoint's `subbed` arrives. The bound is judged for a wake that meets §F's six conditions, each read from the lines: no catch-up line; at most six `subbed` lines for the link; `d`, `h`, `m` and `J` inside §F's arithmetic; on the lane that was adopted, no `attempt failed` line between its target's first answer and its `up`; where that lane is direct, no `lane direct: spent (the bucket …)` line; and no `wrote <n> act message(s)` line at the link's `up`, or one of at most 4,096 bytes. Then each link's `up over the <carrier> carrier` line and each of its subscriptions' `subbed` lines come within 1 s of that carrier's `reachable`, the start of the probe's try: the machine's direct port for the direct carrier, the hub for the relay, whichever carrier came up. The time from that try's end is recorded beside it. A miss inside the conditions is a fail, recorded with what was measured and put to the owner. A wake that misses a condition is neither a pass nor a fail of the bound: it is recorded with the line that shows which, the hour is run once more, and a second such wake is put to the owner. Each session stream is answered from the cursor it asked, by the machine's own line, with no `dropped a repeated event` line and no event missing against the machine's journal; one answered `snapshot` or `missing` is recorded as that, with its cause. Each feed is answered with its snapshot, as RL3 designs a feed. Recorded beside every reading: `d`, `h` and `m`; `J` for each stream, the milliseconds of the machine's `sub` line, with the rows its journal read took (`host.log`'s line), since `J` is not bounded by design and §F's table says how far each carrier carries it; the count of subscriptions; and the time of each subscription's first event or snapshot after `subbed` (the link's line), which is when its content resumed. A lane B stream whose `subbed` is late by its `J`, on a journal past §F's table, misses the third condition and is recorded as that. A lane A stream is recorded with its mode: after the hour it is a snapshot, and its content comes when sminos sends it, at the first-snapshot line's time. The org feed's `connected` and last listings page and the project socket's `caught up` are recorded against the hub's `reachable` and §G's target, and are not held to 1 s |
| 8a, 8b two clients | L | one mini session open in the MacBook's MAWS and the mini's; a question answered from each side in turn. The mini's side is read and answered in its window by the architect through the `cua` device; mini-ops stamps the session's `session.events` beside it | both show the same turn's text growing; each answer settles the other side's card, and the mini's events hold each answer once |
| 9a, 9b, 9c the trust cases | G, T, C | the desk track's trust run on staging on F. 9a, an action: one placed by the hub. 9a, an event: an honest hub cannot place one and RL's acceptance list holds no such case, so it is the gate case of `test/endpoint/rl10/conversation.json`, "arriving events: verified and annotated with their signer's key id, a forged one rejected up and its position spent" (in `--gates all` on F), and on staging a forged upload sent to the hub. 9b: a request card altered after its digest, and a `session.answer` sent with a changed `requestDigest`. 9c: a device removed and then used. Cited beside them: O10's production run (Decision Log 145) and version 24's adoption (263, 264) | each is refused with its reason: the forged upload `bad-signature`, the changed answer `digest-mismatch` with nothing run. No check alters an event from the hub's own position; that is a tracker row at M5, and the owner was told on 2026-11-10 |
| 9x the admin device | C, L | Decision Logs 263 and 264; the Org page read on F | one root, this Mac's; "This Mac signs the org state." |
| 10a to 10c tools with no app | L | on `dc-linux171-1e68`, through production's unit over `machine.sock`, with no app subscribed to the session (both Macs' apps off it): a script (`apps/desk-hub/deploy/vm/o15-tools.mjs`) starts a lane B session on a real model, in a folder the machine already trusts (the script accepts no workspace trust, and a `needs_trust` answer is a fail), names it with `session.rename`, tells it to call `artifact_screenshot` and `artifact_export`, and stops it at the end; `session.capabilities`, read after `session.create` and again after the turn, answers 10c. The owner's display on the VM is watched during the run, and the watch is told to expect the render host's own unit (`electron --headless --render-host` under `xvfb-run`, on a private Xvfb), which opens no window on that display. The model does the work and the script only drives the session, which is how Decision Log 169's ruling reads. Not the replay harness: its scripted mode needs `MAWS_ENGINE_REPLAY=1` in the engine host's environment, which production's unit does not set and will not be given | a PNG and a PDF come back; the capability is offered, or refused with its reason before any call; the record carries the model and the count of turns; the render host and its Xvfb are gone after the quiet period, and nothing of the run is left on the VM |
| 11a owners on shared rows | C | Decision Log 263's step 7, on staging at cf30dafdd. Production has no session with two owners, and M2 does not touch the rows | cited |
| 11b a `say` carries its owners | L | a `sminos say` from a MacBook session to one on the mini and one on the VM | each receiver reads the owner's label, `verified`, signed by the machine |
| 11c drawn as a peer message | L, G, T | the receiving session's pane and the project's Channel tab read in the MacBook's MAWS, which is also O8's item 7 (Decision Log 264) and the migrated-history label's check (Decision Log 155). The desk track's staging run with the second member (row 12) includes a `say` from the second member's session to a first member's session, read in the receiver's transcript on its machine. The owner's drawing in a built app is case G3 of `e2e/agent-tools.spec.ts` (group `e2e:agents`, fixture `events/agents-peer-owners`), in `--gates all` on F | the message shows as that agent's. Production has one member, who owns every session alone, so by Decision Log 111 no owner is drawn there, and that is the pass; the owner's drawing is proved by M2b's test (§J), drawn in a built app by G3, and read on staging, where the receiver's transcript row ends `, owned by` the second member and sminos's stored line reads `verified`. No built app is signed in as a second member, so that drawing is not seen live with two members: a tracker row at M5. | 13a to 13c upgrades mid-turn | S, G | the staged session across the swap (M3), which restarts `mawsd`, replaces the app and hands the engine host over; `e2e:cross-build`. On the mini mini-ops stages it through `machine.sock` with one allow rule, so that a single request is open at the swap; after the swap the session shows exactly that one open request, and once it is answered its events hold the input once, the permission once and the shell's completion once. The swap crosses one build within a minor; a minor's crossing rests on the gate's runs (Decision Logs 209, 217) | M3's conditions |

| 15a to 15c the local boundary | G, L | the socket suites in `--gates all`; on each machine, every socket under the profile listed with its owner and mode, and the listening TCP ports of `mawsd` and its hosts | every socket of MAWS's own is the owner's, mode 0600 in a 0700 directory, none a symlink (a socket gets its mode right after it listens, and the 0700 directory covers that moment). Expected beside them while the app runs, and gone while it is quit: Chromium's single-instance files, `<profile>/SingletonSocket` (a symlink to a 0755 socket in a 0700 directory under the temp folder), `SingletonLock` and `SingletonCookie`, which are Electron's and not among the sockets acceptance 15 binds. On the VM the X sockets under `/tmp/.X11-unix` are outside the profile and are not MAWS's to bind: `X0` is root's Xorg, started with an auth file, and the render host's own Xvfb (`:99`, from `xvfb-run` with its own auth file) lives only while a render runs and for its quiet period after; both are read and recorded. The VM's read is `apps/desk-hub/deploy/vm/o15-sockets.sh`, which flags any loopback listener, since it cannot tell whether a sign-in is running; a flagged one is judged by hand. No listening loopback port but the connectors' sign-in listener while a sign-in runs. **The owner's time.** What only the owner can do: the hub deploy; the swap's approval; the sitting on the MacBook, in two parts: Part A, about 2 h 10 min, which the agent drives alone once the owner says go (the rows marked L that need no Touch ID), and Part B, about 70 minutes with the owner within reach, whose Touch IDs fall in its first ten minutes (5a, 5b, then 4a to 4l and 4q); the quiet window of about 45 minutes (14a to 14c, the repair window's live check, then the bench with its four Touch IDs: pairing the bench app, enrolling `vm-bench`, dropping it and removing the bench app's device); the asleep hour (7); the verdict. The owner agreed to all three sittings on 2026-11-10 (Decision Log 3); their times are set with the owner after the swap.

### M5 — The record and the verdict

**At its end.** P5's "Outcomes and retrospective" holds: the table of 67 results with each one's date, build and evidence; what each child delivered against P5's purpose; what is carried, each with its tracker row; the lessons.
