The tracking map's O15 row reads done, and P5's Decision Log has the closing entries. The registry's execution document records F in its Progress, which releases its constraint 1.

**Decisions.**

- *The risk row on the org root's key is corrected, not closed.* It reads today "Production's org root is a software key until O8". Since O8 M8 the root is this Mac's step-up key, and it is still a software key: a P-256 private JWK in `<userData>/device/keys.json` (directory 0700, file 0600, excluded from Time Machine where the exclusion succeeds), beside the device's channel key. Touch ID gates the app's signing flow, not the key: a process running as the owner that can read the profile can read both keys. The row says that, with what bounds it (FileVault, the paper recovery key offline) and what would end it (keys bound to the Secure Enclave, which need a build signed with a Developer ID, deferred with it). A tracker row carries it.
- *Tracker rows written here:* the older-`mawsd` render and delivery answers in main, to retire once every `mawsd` the app can meet advertises both capabilities; the two retired settings fields, to drop at the next client settings version; the idle bound's re-derivation, which now needs the build before M2 as its comparator; the key custody row above. From M2b: `mawsd`'s own hub link after a wake (noticed within one 15 s heartbeat, then its backoff); `remote.gap` still not drawn (row #407, the renderer shows no gap on a resume that lost a range, stays open); `raceDirect`'s losers that open after the race are charged by the listener and not by the app's bucket, whose right mirror is a bucket per address family; the project socket's wake path has unit tests and no case in the built app, since the e2e scene runs the development session; sminos's frame lets an alias that holds `, owned by …` read as a label on an event sminos left unlabelled, so MAWS's drawn owner on a peer message is as trustworthy as sminos's frame and no more until sminos carries the alias as a string literal where it could be mistaken (the fix is the desk track's, after its M3; the sminos version is recorded on the row when it ships, and §J's parse reads both forms already); the hub spends about 190 ms on the Access check and the Registry round trip of every request, which is most of the relay carrier's share of the wake budget (the desk track's to shorten).
- *Items handed to O15 by name* are each closed in Outcomes: the migrated-history label (11c's row); RL10's projects (the registry); O8's item 7 (11c's row); the admin device (9x); O17 (landed whole, Decision Logs 209 and 210); the MacBook's cold idle reading (14a, 14b); O4's Keychain adoption (1d's row); Decision Log 161's field check (answered by Decision Log 239's swap counts and M3's); O14 B2's step 9, the owner's look at the hub's logs of 2026-10-29, whose failure #345 fixed and whose logs are past retention, put to the owner to close.
- *P5 §2's "Two composition roots" paragraph* gets one dated sentence that the embedded root was deleted, with the Decision Log's number. Children's documents are history and are not edited.
- *The verdict is asked from 2026-11-14*, a week after the swap that began the owner's week (Decision Log 249), through `mcp__sminos__AskUserQuestion`, and recorded in the owner's words.

## Concrete Steps

**M1 and M2, the executor.** One branch, `p5-o15`, in a worktree of the architect's checkout:

    cd /Users/new/.local/share/maws-architect/maws-arch
    git fetch && git worktree add .claude/worktrees/p5-o15 -b p5-o15 origin/master
    cd .claude/worktrees/p5-o15 && pnpm install

Locally, only a focused run that starts no Electron and checks no types, one at a time (constraint 6):

    pnpm exec eslint <one file>
    pnpm vitest run --project node <one test file>

Everything else through the gate script, from the worktree, the typecheck, the whole lint and the unit suite included. Class 1a alone is cheap and may run at any time, and runs green on a head before any of the Mac runs below:

    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates static,unit,probes --class1a linux --runner macos --peer none --label p5-o15

The runs below hold the one CI Mac, the mini, for up to 70 minutes, and the measured groups need it quiet. Before each, tell the architect, who clears the time with CI-opt:

    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates all --class1a linux --runner macos --label p5-o15-all
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates packaged --runner macos --label p5-o15-pkg
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates e2e:artifacts-perf,e2e:daemon-idle,e2e:local-latency-daemon --runner macos --peer none --label p5-o15-measured
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates e2e:cross-build --runner macos --peer 63443a3bd --label p5-o15-cross

The gate script runs the two-build check only between trees a wire minor apart, so its peer is 63443a3bd, the newest master commit at 3.32 while this tree is at 3.33; if this branch takes a minor (constraint 4), the peer is cf30dafdd instead. Each run prints every job's conclusion and ends `ci-gate: run <id> success`. A red job's report is under `/tmp/maws-review/gates/`. What M2's cases show, in `e2e/repair.spec.ts`'s own words once written:

    the daemon cannot start: the repair window, and nothing else
    the daemon can start again: Reinstall and Start or Try Again opens the app
    Open Login Items, then the window's focus, tries once
    closing the repair window quits MAWS
    a profile saved on the embedded shape opens on mawsd with everything present

The executor's report goes to `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-report.md`. It opens a pull request to master and does not merge it; the architect lands it.

**M2b, its own executor.** A second branch, `p5-o15-wake`, from master, beside `p5-o15` (the gate labels below keep the name `p5-o15-m2b`):

    cd /Users/new/.local/share/maws-architect/maws-arch
    git fetch && git worktree add .claude/worktrees/p5-o15-wake -b p5-o15-wake origin/master
    cd .claude/worktrees/p5-o15-wake && pnpm install

Locally the same rule as M1 and M2 (constraint 6): eslint on one file or vitest's node project on one test file, one at a time, and nothing else. Class 1a after every commit that touches TypeScript, and green on a head before any Mac run:

    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates static,unit,probes --class1a linux --runner macos --peer none --label p5-o15-m2b

The three Mac runs, each told to the architect first, who clears the time with CI-opt:

    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates all --class1a linux --runner macos --label p5-o15-m2b-all
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates packaged --runner macos --label p5-o15-m2b-pkg
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh . --gates e2e:remote-latency --runner macos --peer none --label p5-o15-m2b-measured

The measured run prints the first test's lines as today. The new test writes `test-results/remote-wake.json` and prints one line for each carrier: the time from the restore to the link's `up` and to its last `subbed`, the time from the hub's restore to the feed's `connected` and last listings page, and the counts of upgrades and of feed opens. Its title, once written:

    a wake after a silent cut: every subscription answered within 1 s of the restore, one upgrade, nothing lost or repeated, per carrier

The executor's report goes to `docs/doperpowers/plans/2026-11-10-p5-o15-m2b-report.md`. It opens a pull request to master and does not merge it; the architect lands it. Whichever of the two branches lands second merges master first and keeps both sides' edits of `src/main/app.ts`.

**M3, the architect with the desk track, mini-ops and the owner.**

    # the architect, in the architect's checkout at F
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh <checkout> --gates all --class1a linux --runner macos --peer 63443a3bd --label o15-final   # a minor apart, so the plan adds e2e:cross-build
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh <checkout> --gates packaged --runner macos --label o15-final-pkg
    ./scripts/ci-gate.sh <checkout> --gates e2e:local-latency-daemon,e2e:daemon-idle,e2e:daemon-idle-render,e2e:daemon-idle-session-host,e2e:daemon-idle-enrolled,e2e:remote-latency --runner macos --peer none --label o15-final-measured

    # the owner, in a Terminal window, never an agent
    zsh apps/desk-hub/deploy/production-run.sh checkout     # prints the deploy command for F
    zsh apps/desk-hub/deploy/production-run.sh wait <F's first 8 characters>

The swap follows the earlier swaps' steps (P5 Decision Logs 249, 256, 259, 262), with M3's two stagings before it and its reads after it.

**M4, row 7e's probe** (§K). On the MacBook, started before the sleep and left running; and for 30 s after the wake on each machine, with the hub as its one target, for `m`:

    E=~/.local/share/maws-architect/o15-evidence/7e
    node test/support/wake-probe.mjs watch --target hub=<the hub's host>:443 --target mini=<its tailnet IPv4>:47830 --target vm=<its tailnet IPv4>:47830 --https https://<the hub's host>/ --out $E/probe
    # after the wake; <t> is the time of main.log's [wake] line
    node test/support/wake-probe.mjs read $E/probe --after <t> --at <the time of each link's last subbed line>
    node test/support/wake-probe.mjs extract "$HOME/Library/Application Support/MAWS/logs/main.log" --since <t> > $E/main-lines.txt
    pmset -g log | grep -E 'Wake from|DarkWake'      # the wake's own time, to the second

`read` prints for each target the first counted try that succeeded, with its start and end, the time from each to every `--at`, and the median connect time after the wake; tries that began before `<t>` are listed as excluded. `extract` is run the same way over each machine's `mawsd.log` and `host.log`, on the machine; only what it prints is copied. `https.jsonl` is read only if the hub's probes failed: a GET that succeeded while they failed is Decision Log 6 (k)'s risk seen.

**M4.** Each row of the table is run as its "What is done" says, by the role its method names. Evidence goes to `~/.local/share/maws-architect/o15-evidence/<claim>/`, and each row's result (time, build, pass or fail, one line of what was seen) goes into this document's Progress as it is obtained.

## Interfaces and Dependencies

### §A. The launch outcome (`src/main/hosts/daemon.ts`)

    export type LaunchShape = { daemon: 'unit' | 'private' };
    export type UnavailableKind = 'no-unit' | 'not-loaded' | 'install-refused' | 'not-started';
    export interface Unavailable { kind: UnavailableKind; why: string }   // why: the system's or mawsd's own words, one line
    export type LaunchOutcome = { ok: true; launch: LaunchShape } | { ok: false; unavailable: Unavailable };

`resolveLaunch` answers a `LaunchOutcome`, and its input no longer carries the setting.

| Profile | Build | The main profile's unit | Outcome |
|---|---|---|---|
| not the main one | any | not read | `private` |
| main | packaged | any | the unit is installed or repaired to run this bundle, asked to start, and must welcome a hello within `UNIT_READY_MS`: then `unit`. Anything else is one of the four cases below |
| main | unpackaged | installed and loaded | asked to start, and must welcome: then `unit`. No welcome: `not-started` |
| main | unpackaged | installed, not loaded | `not-loaded` |
| main | unpackaged | not installed, or no unit manager | `no-unit` |

A test or harness launch on the main profile exits before the table, as today. The first row is today's and starts nothing (M2, "What the window does not cover").

*A start that does not end in a welcome, on a packaged build* (Decision Log 6). The rule is written so that it holds whichever step macOS refuses, and so does not depend on what one macOS version does to a loaded agent that is turned off under Login Items.

| Case | What happened | Outcome | `why` |
|---|---|---|---|
| (i) | the install or repair was refused: `launchctl bootstrap` answered non-zero | `install-refused` | launchctl's words |
| (ii) | the start was refused: `launchctl kickstart` answered non-zero | `install-refused` | launchctl's words |
| (iii) | the start reported success, no welcome came within `UNIT_READY_MS`, `mawsd` wrote nothing since this attempt began, and no live process holds `mawsd.lock` | `install-refused` | `macOS loaded mawsd but did not run it` |
| (iv) | no welcome, and `mawsd` wrote since this attempt began or a live process holds its lock | `not-started` | the welcome wait's last answer, as today |

Whether `mawsd` wrote is read with no clock. Before the install or repair is asked, and so before the start, main reads the size and the inode of `<profile>/mawsd/mawsd.log` and of `<profile>/mawsd/mawsd.out`, a missing file counting as a value of its own; after the wait it reads them again, and any difference in either is a write. A `mawsd` that the bootstrap itself started (`RunAtLoad`) and that wrote and died before its lock is therefore (iv), never (iii) (Decision Log 12). A `mawsd` that starts writes its `starting` line to `mawsd.log` before it takes its lock (`src/mawsd/daemon.ts`), and what it prints before that goes to `mawsd.out`, the unit's standard output and error. The lock is read as main reads it today (`readLock` and `isAlive` of `src/engine-host/supervisor-files.ts`); it tells a `mawsd` that was already running and is silent, which is (iv), from a job macOS never ran. Nothing of `launchctl print`'s text is parsed and no state line is added to (iii)'s `why`: the unit's code reads only that command's exit status (`loaded` in `src/mawsd/units/launchd.ts`). MAWS's unit plist carries `AssociatedBundleIdentifiers` [`dev.maws.app`] (`launchdPlist`, same file), which is why Login Items shows one MAWS row with one switch for the app and its daemon.

### §B. The repair window (`src/main/repair/`, `src/preload/repair.ts`, `src/renderer/repair.html`, `src/renderer/src/repair/`)

    export interface RepairState {
      kind: UnavailableKind;
      why: string;
      packaged: boolean;      // decides the retry action's label
      attempting: boolean;    // an attempt is running: the actions are disabled
      log: { path: string; lines: string[] } | null;
    }
    export type RepairAction = 'retry' | 'open-login-items' | 'reveal-log' | 'quit';

The preload exposes `window.repair`: `state(): Promise<RepairState>`, `onState(listener): () => void`, `act(action: RepairAction): void`, over three channels of its own, `repair.state`, `repair.changed` and `repair.act`. Main's side is one function that takes the first failure and a function that attempts the launch again, resolves with the `LaunchShape` of the first attempt that succeeds, and quits MAWS when the window is closed or `quit` is acted.

`log` is the last 20 lines of `<profile>/mawsd/mawsd.log`, each cut at 300 characters, read at every state change; of `mawsd.out` when the log has none; null when neither has any. `open-login-items` opens `x-apple.systempreferences:com.apple.LoginItems-Settings.extension`. `reveal-log` shows the file in Finder.

The words. The window's title is `MAWS`. While an attempt runs the page shows `Starting mawsd…`.

| Kind | Headline | Body |
|---|---|---|
| `no-unit` | This development build has no machine daemon for your main profile | MAWS runs its sessions, terminals and index in a background process, mawsd, which only an installed MAWS.app sets up for the main profile. Run this build on a profile of its own with `pnpm dev:scratch`, or install MAWS.app and open this build again. |
| `not-loaded` | MAWS's machine daemon is turned off | mawsd is installed, but macOS has not loaded it. In System Settings › General › Login Items & Extensions, under Allow in the Background, turn MAWS on, then come back to this window. |
| `install-refused` | macOS did not let MAWS set up or start its machine daemon | MAWS runs its sessions, terminals and index in a background process, mawsd, and could not load or start it: {why}. If MAWS is turned off in System Settings › General › Login Items & Extensions, under Allow in the Background, turn it on, then come back to this window. |
| `not-started` | MAWS's machine daemon did not start | macOS loaded mawsd, but it did not answer within 10 seconds: {why}. Its last log lines are below. |

| Kind | Actions, the first the default |
|---|---|
| `no-unit` | Quit MAWS; Try Again |
| `not-loaded` | Open Login Items…; Try Again; Quit MAWS |
| `install-refused` | Open Login Items…; Reinstall and Start on a packaged build, Try Again on a development build; Quit MAWS |
| `not-started` | Reinstall and Start on a packaged build, Try Again on a development build; Show Log in Finder; Quit MAWS |

Reinstall and Start and Try Again are the same action, `retry`: on a packaged build §A's table installs or repairs the unit before it starts it, and the label says so.

### §C. The e2e fault, the five cases and acceptance 3's scene

`MAWS_E2E_UNIT_FAULT=<absolute path>` is honoured only when `MAWS_E2E=1` and the app is unpackaged, on a profile that is not the main one. While the file exists and its trimmed content is one of the four kinds, the launch's outcome is that failure with `why` `e2e fault`; otherwise the row's own outcome, `private`. A packaged app ignores it. The fault replaces the table's answer, so it proves the window and what main does and does not compose around it. The table's own rows (the unit installed, refused, started, welcomed or silent) are pinned by unit tests over a faked unit runner, as `src/main/hosts/daemon.test.ts` does today, and are seen for real once, in M4's live check of the window. Each of §A's cases (i) to (iv) has its own test there: a `bootstrap` that answers non-zero, a `kickstart` that answers non-zero, a silent start with the two files unchanged and no live lock holder, and a silent start with a file changed or a live holder.

1. *The daemon cannot start.* With the fault `not-started`, the first window is the repair page with that headline; no app window exists; `spine.lock` is held by no process; the profile's `mawsd/` directory holds no lock this launch made.
2. *It can start again.* The fault file removed and the retry action pressed: the repair window closes, the app's window opens, and its session list loads.
3. *Login Items.* With `not-loaded`, Open Login Items… asks for the settings URL (read through the suite's record of external opens where one exists, else pinned in the unit test alone), and the window's next focus starts exactly one attempt.
4. *Closing quits.* The repair window closed: the process exits with code 0.
5. *A saved `embedded`* (`e2e/settings.spec.ts`). A scratch profile whose client `settings.json` holds `supervisor.shape: embedded` beside three other settings off their defaults: the launch runs on `mawsd`, whose pid holds `spine.lock`; the three settings read as written; after the first settings write the file's `shape` reads `daemon` and its version is still 21.

*Acceptance 3's data.* A hand-built profile cannot prove it: `test/support/o2-profile.ts` seeds the index alone, and the library's first reconcile with the engine host deletes rows the host does not hold. The proof is a cross-build scene that M2 adds to `e2e/cross-build.spec.ts`, on the embedded peer M1 keeps (Decision Log 14): the peer, a real build with the embedded shape, makes a session, an artifact, a draft and a layout of more than one tab on a profile and quits; the tested build opens that profile on `mawsd`. The scene asserts, after the library's first sync and again after a relaunch of the tested build: the session listed, the artifact in the library and openable, the draft, the layout's tabs, and no index rebuild in `mawsd.log`. It needs a peer that still has the embedded shape and skips, saying so, against a later one; M5 files its retirement.

### §D. The wake notice (`src/main/net/wake.ts`)

    export const WAKE_TICK_MS = 1_000;
    export const WAKE_DROP_MS = 10_000;        // twice PING_MS[0] of src/endpoint/channels.ts; a test holds the two
    export const WAKE_CLOCK_GAP_MS = 60_000;   // the least time between two notices the timer raises

    export interface WakeNotice { sleptMs: number; source: 'resume' | 'clock' }
    export interface WakeSource {
      onWake(listener: (notice: WakeNotice) => void): () => void;
      onUnlock(listener: () => void): () => void;
      close(): void;
    }
    type PowerEvent = 'suspend' | 'resume' | 'unlock-screen';
    export function createWakeSource(deps: {
      power: { on(event: PowerEvent, listener: () => void): unknown; off(event: PowerEvent, listener: () => void): unknown };   // Electron's powerMonitor
      now?(): number;
      log(line: string): void;
    }): WakeSource & { inject(sleptMs: number): void };

| Event | What the source does |
|---|---|
| `suspend` | remembers its time |
| `resume` | `sleptMs` is the time since the remembered `suspend`, or since the last tick when none was heard; forgets the `suspend`; takes now as the last tick; tells `{ sleptMs, source: 'resume' }` |
| a tick, every `WAKE_TICK_MS`, on a timer that does not hold the process | `late` is now, less the last tick, less `WAKE_TICK_MS`; takes now as the last tick; if `late` is at least `WAKE_DROP_MS` and the timer raised no notice within `WAKE_CLOCK_GAP_MS`, forgets the `suspend` and tells `{ sleptMs: late, source: 'clock' }` |
| `unlock-screen` | tells the unlock listeners |
| `inject(sleptMs)` | takes now as the last tick and tells `{ sleptMs, source: 'clock' }`: §I's seam calls it |

Every notice writes `[wake] slept <ms> ms (<source>)`. A consumer drops its sockets when `sleptMs` is at least `WAKE_DROP_MS` and makes today's probe when it is less. The timer reads the wall clock's lateness and no second clock, because whether Node's monotonic clock advances through a macOS sleep is not something this repository shows, and a timer that was due during the sleep fires when the process runs again either way. A main process that a loaded runner stalls for 10 s raises a `clock` notice in the suites too: the reconnect it causes is the designed one, and its `[wake]` line says what happened when a case is read afterwards.

Unit (`wake.test.ts`, a fake power source and a fake clock): a `resume` after a `suspend` tells the time between them; a `resume` with no `suspend` tells the time since the last tick; a tick 10 s late tells one `clock` notice, and a `resume` that follows it within the second tells a short sleep; a tick late by less tells nothing; two late ticks within a minute tell one notice; `unlock-screen` is passed on; `close()` removes the three listeners and the timer.

### §E. The burst window and the probes (`src/main/net/reach.ts`)

    export const REACH_WINDOW_MS = 60_000;
    export const PROBE_STAGGER_MS = 100;
    export const PROBE_CONNECT_MS = 3_000;
    export const WINDOW_FLOOR_MS = 10_000;   // §F row 8, §G rule 3

    export interface Reach {
      /** Opens the window for REACH_WINDOW_MS from now; called while it is open, the count starts again. */
      openWindow(reason: 'wake' | 'network'): void;
      windowOpen(): boolean;
      /**
       * true at the first TCP connect to `target`'s host and port that succeeds while the window is open; false at once
       * when the window is not open, false when it closes first or `signal` aborts.
       */
      reachable(target: string, signal?: AbortSignal): Promise<boolean>;
      close(): void;
    }
    export function createReach(deps: {
      connect?(address: string, port: number, signal: AbortSignal): Promise<void>;   // node:net's connect by default: resolves at 'connect', rejects at 'error'; an abort destroys the socket
      lookup?(host: string): Promise<readonly { address: string; family: number }[]>;   // node:dns/promises' lookup with all: true by default
      now?(): number;
      log(line: string): void;
    }): Reach;
    export const NO_REACH: Reach;   // its window never opens

- A target is a URL (`ws:`, `wss:`, `http:`, `https:`). Its host and port, 80 or 443 where it names none, are its key: the hub's URL as a link, the feed and a project socket give it is one key and one prober.
- While the window is open and someone waits on a key, a probe starts at once and then every `PROBE_STAGGER_MS`. A probe is one TCP connect given `PROBE_CONNECT_MS`, so at most `PROBE_CONNECT_MS / PROBE_STAGGER_MS`, thirty, are in flight for a key. The first to connect is destroyed at once, the key's other probes are destroyed, every waiter of the key is answered `true`, and the key is probed no more until someone waits on it again.
- When a key's last waiter leaves (its signal aborted) the key's probes are destroyed. When the window closes every waiter is answered `false`.
- A probe connects to an address, never to a name, so that a resolver that stalls (the state right after a wake, before the network is back) cannot pile up lookups that no probe's deadline can cancel on libuv's thread pool, which main's file work and the real dial's own lookup share. A host that is an IP literal is probed as it is. A name is looked up by the reach, with at most one lookup of it outstanding at any instant across rounds and windows; a round of any key of that host joins it. The round's probes take the answer's addresses in turn, the two families alternating, from the answer on, so a v4-only or a v6-only path still answers. The name is looked up again at the next tick after a failed lookup when the round has no address, and once a probe to each address of the answer has failed, the old addresses probed meanwhile. A lookup still outstanding when its round ends finishes, and its answer is dropped (Decision Log 32).
- Lines: `[reach] window open for 60 s (<reason>)`; for a key, once per round of waiting, `[reach] <host>:<port>: reachable after <n> probes, <ms> ms` or `[reach] <host>:<port>: window closed after <n> probes, none connected (<the last error's words, §H>)`; a window that closes before any probe started, with no lookup error, writes `(no answer from the lookup of <host>)` as the reason. No line per probe.

The interval is 100 ms because a probe that finds nothing costs one unanswered SYN and a probe that connects ends the probing: nothing argues for the slower end of the range, and 100 ms is a tenth of the bound at worst. A probe is given 3 s, three times the app's standing judgment of a direct open (`DIRECT_DIAL_MS`), so that a slow or lossy path still answers one; the thirty in flight that this allows are thirty sockets that carry nothing. A path whose TCP connect takes over 3 s answers no probe: the floor gives it today's loop 10 s into the window (§F, row 8), and the window's close leaves today's backoff.

Unit (`reach.test.ts`, a fake `connect` and a fake clock): with no window `reachable` answers `false` at once and connects nothing; with one, a probe at once and one each 100 ms while someone waits, never a thirty-first in flight, a probe that hangs destroyed at 3 s; the first connect answers every waiter of its key, destroys the rest and stops the key's probing; two URLs of one host and port share a prober, and a URL with no port takes its scheme's; the last waiter's abort stops the probing; the window's close answers `false`; `openWindow` while open starts the 60 s again. A fake lookup besides: lookups that stall for the whole window leave at most one outstanding per host at every instant; a name that resolves late is probed from then on; an IP literal takes no lookup; a name that resolves to both families is probed on both.

### §F. The link in the window (`src/main/machines/remote/`)

    // RemoteLinkDeps gains
    reach?: Reach;                               // NO_REACH by default: no window opens and the link is today's
    directTarget?(url: string): string | null;   // where an advertised direct address is probed: itself by default; null: never probed
    // dialDirect and openRelay each gain a last parameter, signal?: AbortSignal: aborted, the socket is terminated and the open rejects
    // handshake gains a last parameter, options?: { timeoutMs?: number }: connectChannel's own, each handshake message's deadline
    // RemoteTiming gains
    WINDOW_OPEN_MS: number;                      // 2_000: a relay socket's open by a lane (10 s for today's attempt)
    WINDOW_HANDSHAKE_MS: number;                 // 2_000: each handshake message of a lane's attempt (10 s for today's)
    LANE_BACKOFF_MS: readonly number[];          // [100, 250, 500, 1_000, 2_000], jittered by BACKOFF_JITTER
    LANE_ATTEMPTS: number;                       // 6: a lane's attempts from one entry of the link into lanes to the next
    // TokenBucket.holds gains a parameter: holds(tokens = 1)
    // ActTable.onCurrent returns { messages: number; bytes: number }: everything it wrote on the new channel, acts sent or resealed and queries alike
    // RemoteLink gains
    wake(sleptMs: number): void;
    // EndpointRecords gains
    cached(ref: MachineRef, signedIn: SignedIn): Promise<Signed<EndpointRecord> | null>;   // the held record if it verifies now (memory, then the file); no fetch

`src/main/app.ts` replaces `directTarget` in the two places it replaces `dialDirect`: the development session's `directMap` maps it to the same address, and the relay-only bench makes it `null`.

*Three modes.* A link is at any moment in exactly one. Nothing below changes `link.ts` in the first.

| Mode | When | What runs |
|---|---|---|
| *today* | no window is open, and no lane attempt is still in flight | `link.ts` as it is: `startAttempt`, `connect`, `finish`, `scheduleRetry`, `probe`, `recheckDirect`. One attempt at most, as today |
| *lanes* | a window is open and the floor's time has not come for this link since it last entered *lanes* (at an opening, or by row 12); or it has come, or the window has just closed, and a lane attempt is still in flight (rows 8 and 9) | the two lanes, below. Today's loop does not run: nothing calls `startAttempt`, and `retryTimer` is cleared on entry. One direct attempt and one relay attempt at most |
| *today with probe triggers* | a window is open, the floor's time has come for this link since it last entered *lanes*, and no lane attempt is in flight | today's loop alone, exactly as in *today*, with one addition: a lane that has had no answer since the window's last opening goes on waiting on its targets, and its first answer on a link with no channel returns the link to *lanes* (row 12). No lane attempt runs |

So the lanes and today's loop are never concurrent on a link. In *lanes* the direct lane is the only direct dialler and the relay lane the only opener of relay sockets; in the other two modes today's `connect` is. `viaDirect`'s check of the bucket before the open and its charge after it therefore stay as they are: there is one direct dialler at a time, as there is today.

*A link with a channel.* The modes say how a link without a channel seeks one. A link with a current channel seeks nothing in any mode, and today's recheck of the direct carrier (`recheckDirect`) runs on a relay channel as it does today, row 11 apart: it is then the one direct dialler. If the relay channel is lost inside a window while a recheck's dial is in flight, `lost` seeks into *lanes* and the relay lane starts, and the direct lane starts nothing until that dial has ended: a channel it completes is the reconnect, as today (`recheckDirect`'s `current === null` case), and a failure counts as the direct lane's (row 3).

*To seek* stands wherever `link.ts` calls `startAttempt` today: in `lost`, `probe`, `lift`, `benchRelease`, the retry timer, `finish`, the end of `recheckDirect` and the link's creation. In *today* and in *today with probe triggers* it is `startAttempt`, unchanged. In *lanes* it starts the lanes if they are not running and otherwise does nothing: they are already trying. A held link (`benchHold`), a refused one and a closed one seek nothing, as `startAttempt` returns for them today.

*Opening the window.* `wake(sleptMs)` and `probe('network')` open it (`reach.openWindow`), and each opening starts the link over, whatever mode it was in:

- `wake` at `WAKE_DROP_MS` or over: every attempt in flight is aborted (row 10) and today's `attempting` and `again` cleared with it, the cached relay socket is closed, a current channel is closed with the reason `wake` (a loss like any other: `lost`), and the link enters *lanes* with both lanes' counts at zero and the floor's timer started. One line: `wake after <ms> ms: dropped <the <carrier> channel | no channel>[, the relay socket][, <n> attempt(s) in flight]`.
- `wake` under `WAKE_DROP_MS`, and `probe('network')`, with a channel: today's ping and, on the relay, today's recheck, exactly as `probe` does them now. A direct lane that row 6 left running is ended first, its attempt aborted: after an opening, a link with a channel has no lane, and today's recheck is its way to the direct carrier. The window is open if that ping then loses the channel, and `lost` then seeks into *lanes*.
- The same two with no channel: as the long wake less the channel. Attempts are aborted, the cached relay socket closed, and *lanes* entered afresh.
- A link that is `refused`, `closed` or held does none of it.

A probe that does not open the window (`focus`, `unlock`, `person`) is today's in every mode, with one reading: on a link with no channel in *lanes* it starts nothing, since the lanes are running. Each probe writes `probe <reason>: <ping sent | seeking | refused | closed>`. The network watch in `source.ts` goes on calling `probe('network')` on an address change, and `src/main/machines/index.ts` calls every link's `wake` from the wake source and `probeAll('unlock')` from its unlock, and no longer imports `powerMonitor`.

*The lanes.* In *lanes* a link has a direct lane and a relay lane. The direct lane's targets are `directTarget` of every tailnet address of `records.cached(...)`; the relay lane's one target is the hub's URL, when the session has a hub. A lane with no target is spent from the start. Today's `records.resolve` is started beside them and not waited for; a record that comes back with other addresses gives the direct lane its targets. A lane waits on its targets (`reach.reachable`) whenever it has no attempt running. Its attempt is one `connectChannel` (below) on the target that answered. On the direct lane the dial is one upgrade to that address, with no race and no record read. On the relay lane the attempt opens a relay socket of its own, which belongs to that attempt until it is adopted: it is closed when the attempt fails or is aborted, and becomes the link's cached relay socket (`relay`) when the attempt is adopted. A lane's attempt runs under the window's deadlines: `DIRECT_DIAL_MS` for a direct dial as always, `WINDOW_OPEN_MS` for the relay socket's open, `WINDOW_HANDSHAKE_MS` for each handshake message. They are 2 s where today's are 10 s: more than four times the budget's largest share of either step (the relay's open, 3 h + H, is 450 ms at the budget's limit), so an attempt on a path a few times slower than the budget's still completes, and one that hangs above TCP frees its lane in seconds.

| # | Mode | Event | What happens |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | lanes | a lane's target answers `true`; the lane has no attempt running, is in no backoff and is not spent | the lane starts its attempt at once, whatever the other lane is doing. The direct lane does so only if `bucket.holds(2)`; if not, it is spent (row 4) |
| 2 | lanes | a lane's target answers `true` during the lane's backoff | the answer is kept; the lane starts its attempt when the backoff ends, and not later |
| 3 | lanes | a lane's attempt ends failed (an open or a handshake message past its deadline, a refusal, a verdict, a trust generation that moved) | its socket is closed. The lane's count rises; it backs off by `LANE_BACKOFF_MS` at that count and waits on its targets again at once, so a path that returns during the backoff has answered by its end |
| 4 | lanes | a lane is spent: it has made `LANE_ATTEMPTS` attempts since the link last entered *lanes*; or it is the direct lane, `bucket.holds(2)` is false and it has no attempt in flight; or it has no target | it waits on nothing and attempts nothing more until the link next enters *lanes*. When both lanes are spent and the link has no channel, the floor fires now (row 8) |
| 5 | lanes | the direct attempt completes its handshake, no channel current | today's checks (the bench's hold, the trust generation), then `adopt`. The relay attempt, if one runs, is aborted and its socket closed (row 10). Both lanes stop and the floor's timer is cleared |
| 6 | lanes | the relay attempt completes its handshake, no channel current | today's checks, then `adopt`; its relay socket becomes `relay`. The floor's timer is cleared. The relay lane stops. The direct lane goes on: an attempt in flight continues, and the lane goes on waiting and attempting within its count |
| 7 | lanes | the direct attempt completes while a relay channel is current (row 6 came first) | today's switch: the end of `recheckDirect` as it is, with its own checks; the direct channel becomes current and the relay channel is closed. Both lanes stop |
| 8 | lanes | the floor's time: the link has been in *lanes* without a channel for `WINDOW_FLOOR_MS` (the timer below), or row 4 brought it sooner | from now until the link next enters *lanes*, a lane that has had an answer since the window's last opening starts no attempt: its backoff timer is cleared and its wait cancelled. A lane that has had none goes on waiting on its targets, from this moment and not only once the link has left *lanes* (row 12). A lane attempt in flight is not aborted: it runs to its own end under its window deadlines, and is adopted by rows 5 to 7 or fails by row 3 without a backoff. When none is in flight the link is in *today with probe triggers*: with no channel, `startAttempt` runs and one line is written, `floor after <ms> ms: today's attempts from here (<why>)`, the why being `10 s without a channel` or `both lanes spent` |
| 9 | lanes | the window closes (60 s after its last opening) | no lane attempt starts from now. One in flight runs to its own end under its window deadlines and is adopted by rows 5 to 7 or fails by row 3 without a backoff. When none is in flight the link is in *today*; if it has no channel, `scheduleRetry` runs then, and not before |
| 10 | any | an attempt is aborted: the window opened again, it is today's and the link returned to *lanes* (row 12), the other lane was adopted over direct, the link was refused, closed or held | its signal fires: an open in flight is terminated and a socket or carrier it holds is closed. If its handshake still completes, the channel is closed and nothing is adopted; no lane's state is touched by it |
| 11 | lanes | today's recheck comes due (`recheckTimer`, or a probe on a link up over the relay) while the direct lane is running | `recheckDirect` does not dial: it arms its timer again, as it does today when an attempt is running. The direct lane is the recheck for as long as it runs |
| 12 | lanes past the floor's time, or today with probe triggers | a lane that had had no answer since the window's last opening gets its first: one of its targets answers `true` | with no channel, and for the direct lane only if `bucket.holds(2)`: the link enters *lanes* afresh, as an opening does for a link with no channel, less `reach.openWindow`. Today's attempt, if one is in flight, is aborted (row 10) and `attempting`, `again` and `retryTimer` are cleared with it, and the cached relay socket is closed. A lane attempt in flight, which can only be the other lane's, is not touched. Both lanes' counts are zero and the floor's timer starts again. The answer is the lane's own: it attempts at once by row 1, with no second probe. One line: `lanes again: <host>:<port> answered, <ms> ms after the floor`. With a channel: nothing. For the direct lane without two tokens: nothing but row 4's `spent` line. In every case the lane has had its answer, so a link enters *lanes* by this row at most twice in one opening, once for each lane, and today's loop is put off by at most two more floors |
| 13 | today with probe triggers | the window closes | the waits end, answered `false`; the link is in *today*, and today's loop goes on as it was |

Each event is handled in the turn it arrives, and a lane is marked as attempting before anything is awaited, so two answers in one turn start one attempt on a lane. A lane's count runs from one entry of the link into *lanes*, at an opening or by row 12, to the next: a channel lost and found again in between does not reset it. The floor's timer runs only while the link is in *lanes* with no channel: it starts when the link enters *lanes* with none, or when the link loses its channel in *lanes*, and an adopted channel clears it. The trust check (`checkTrust`) treats a lane's attempt as it treats today's: one begun under an older generation is discarded when it completes, which is row 3. A failed attempt writes `attempt failed (<direct | relay | today's>): <reason>; next in <ms> ms`, once per lane and reason until the next `up`, as the handshake's verdict line is bounded today; an open that failed gives §H's error words as its reason. A lane that is spent writes `lane <direct | relay>: spent (<6 attempts | the bucket holds fewer than two tokens | no target>)`.

*The two arrival orders, and the late one.* Direct first: row 5, and the relay's attempt is aborted; if its handshake completes anyway, row 10 closes it. Relay first: row 6, and the direct attempt, if it completes, takes over by row 7; if it fails, row 3, and the lane tries again within its count; when the lane is spent or the window has closed with no direct attempt in flight, today's recheck timer, armed by `adopt` as always, is what is left. Both in one turn: the first handled is adopted by row 5 or 6 and the second meets a current channel: a direct one is row 7, a relay one is closed with its socket. A new opening aborts every attempt from before it, and a return to *lanes* aborts today's (row 10), so none of those adopts anything. The floor's time and the window's close abort nothing: a lane attempt in flight at either is adopted if it completes (rows 8 and 9), and so is one that a return to *lanes* found in flight.

*An attempt, and catch-up.* A lane's attempt is one `connectChannel` (`apps/org-protocol/src/channel.ts`), unchanged. It is one dial when the two ends hold the same org head. When they do not, as after a machine key or the org state changed during the sleep, the handshake is refused `behind`, each side takes the other's page, and `connectChannel` dials again, up to `MAX_CATCH_UP_CONNECTIONS` (16) connections; that recovery is kept exactly as it is. So "one upgrade and one handshake" is the common case and not a property. On the direct lane every dial, a catch-up's redials included, needs `bucket.holds(2)` and takes a token when its socket opens, as `viaDirect`'s dials do today with one; a redial the bucket does not allow fails the attempt. On the relay lane each redial is one more channel on the attempt's own socket. Each redial writes `attempt (<direct | relay | today's>): catch-up, dial <n>`, so a wake that needed catch-up shows its count. The abort reaches every dial without a change to the protocol package: the attempt's signal goes to `dialDirect` and `openRelay`; the `dial` function the attempt hands `connectChannel` throws when the signal has fired, at the first dial and at each redial, and closes the carrier it returned when the signal fires later; a carrier's close ends the handshake's read (`Carrier.close` in `carriers.ts` tells its end listeners), so `connectChannel` rejects; and a channel that still completes is closed by row 10.

*The bucket.* The listener's per-source bucket is a burst of 3 with one token back every 12 s, and the app's bucket mirrors it. A lane's dial needs two tokens in the bucket, so the direct lane never takes the last token: that one is today's attempt's, after the floor or after the window. From a full bucket that is two charged dials, and one more for each token that has come back, one every 12 s, when the link enters *lanes* again. A dial that never opens its socket takes no token, as today, and is bounded by `LANE_ATTEMPTS`. The bucket holds two tokens after any sleep of 24 s, whatever was spent before it, and after a shorter one unless the link had dialled direct more than once in the half minute before. What the app's bucket does not see (an accepted upgrade whose 101 was lost, `raceDirect`'s losers that opened after the race) is as it is today and is M5's tracker row.

*The floor* is what keeps the window from leaving a link worse off than today's code for long. Before it, the link is without today's loop for 10 s, and for less when both lanes are spent. A lane attempt in flight at the floor's time adds its own deadlines at most and is not aborted: a path that first answers in the tenth second would otherwise have its first attempt cut, and would miss the bound with every condition below holding. After it, the link is today's code, with `raceDirect` and today's 10 s deadlines: no lane, no second dialler. So a path that answers no probe (a TCP connect over 3 s), or one on which a lane's 2 s deadlines are too short, is served by today's loop from then on, inside the window as outside it.

*A path that returns after the floor.* The floor does not end a lane whose path has not come back yet. The window is 60 s because a network can take longer than 10 s to return, and today's attempt cannot make the bound over the relay when the direct path is silent: `connect` gives the direct race its `DIRECT_DIAL_MS`, 1 s, before it opens the relay. So a lane that has had no answer since the window's last opening never stops waiting on its targets while the window is open, and its first answer puts the link in *lanes* afresh (row 12), as a floor open of the feed keeps its wait in §G's rule 3. That holds from the floor's time itself, while another lane's attempt is still running to its end: were the wait cancelled then, a hub that came back a moment after the floor would not be seen until a hanging direct attempt had run out its deadline, and the relay's first attempt, begun only then, would succeed and still be late, with every condition holding (Decision Log 11). Today's loop and the lanes are still never concurrent: while a lane attempt runs to its end today's loop has not started, and where it has, the return aborts today's attempt before the lane starts, as an opening does and for the opening's reason, that an attempt begun before the path returned would hold the link to its deadlines. An attempt of today's that began in the moment between the path's return and the probe's answer is aborted with the rest, at the cost of one dial. A lane that had its answer before the floor, and then failed, brings no return, whichever of its targets answers later: today's backoff is its way, as it is outside a window. So a link enters *lanes* again at most twice in one opening, today's loop is put off by at most two more floors, and the bound does not depend on when in the window a lane's path returns.

*The relay socket.* `lost` closes the cached relay socket when the reason is `timeout` or `wake`, in every mode. In *lanes* there is no cached one: it is closed on entry, and each relay attempt owns its own until adoption.

*The budget, and the wake it is claimed for.* The 1 s is claimed for a wake in which all of these hold, and the same list stands in acceptance 6 and in M4's 7e row:

1. the handshake needs no catch-up page: one dial;
2. the link has at most six subscriptions. Six is two open sessions of one machine, each with its stream and its checkout feed (`src/main/lane-b/remote-checkout.ts`: one beside each stream main holds), and the machine's attention and artifacts feeds (one each for a link); the endpoint allows 64 (`MAX_SUBS`);
3. the round trips and `J` are inside the arithmetic below;
4. on the lane that is adopted, the first attempt after that lane's first probe answer succeeds. A failed attempt costs its own time and a backoff under any design;
5. where the direct lane is the one adopted, the bucket held two tokens when the lane made its attempt, which the paragraph above shows it does after the hour asleep;
6. what the link writes on the new channel ahead of the `sub`s is at most 4 KiB in all. `adopt` calls `acts.onCurrent` before `subs.onCurrent`, and the channel writes in order. `acts.onCurrent` sends or reseals each act still inside its 60 s (`ACT_FRESH_MS`), and one may be as large as the channel's limit, 4 MiB; for each older act still open it sends a query of a few hundred bytes, and nothing limits how many are open. On a slow uplink either can take seconds, and no round trip or count above bounds it. The arithmetic below counts round trips and takes the transfer of small messages as nothing, the handshake's and the `sub`s' own among them, a few KiB in all; 4 KiB ahead of the `sub`s, about a dozen queries, stays within that. So after the hour asleep the condition holds unless more than about a dozen acts are open, and it fails after a shorter sleep during which a large send was waiting. `ActTable.onCurrent` returns the count and the bytes of everything it wrote, acts and queries alike, and `adopt` writes `wrote <n> act message(s), <bytes> bytes, ahead of the subscriptions` when that is not nothing. The order is not changed here: it is the link's behaviour outside any window, and whether the `sub`s can go first is M5's tracker row.

None of the six is about when the path returns: at any time while the window is open, a lane's first answer starts its attempt at once, by row 1 before the floor's time and by row 12 after it.

A wake outside the list is neither a pass nor a fail of the bound. The lines show which condition it missed: the catch-up line, the count of `subbed` lines, the probe's round trips and the machine's `J`, the `attempt failed` line, the `lane direct: spent (the bucket …)` line, the bytes of the `wrote <n> act message(s) …` line. All `sub`s go at once when the channel is adopted (`subs.onCurrent`), behind what `acts.onCurrent` wrote just before them, which the sixth condition holds to 4 KiB; so the last step is one round trip for all of them and then `J`.

| Step | Direct | Relay |
|---|---|---|
| the wait for the next probe to start | ≤ 100 ms | ≤ 100 ms |
| the probe's TCP connect | 1 d | 1 h |
| the open: TCP, on the hub TLS, then the upgrade | 2 d | 3 h + H |
| the RL2 handshake: `h1` for `h2`, then `h3` for the endpoint's first sealed message | 2 d | 2 (h + m) |
| every `sub` for its `subbed`: the round trip | 1 d | 1 (h + m) |
| the two ends' handshake work | P | P |
| the endpoint's work between a `sub` and its `subbed` | J | J |
| in all | 100 ms + 6 d + P + J | 100 ms + 7 h + 3 m + H + P + J |

`d` is the round trip between this Mac and the machine over the tailnet, `h` between this Mac and the hub's edge, `m` between the hub and the machine on the machine's own hub socket. `H` is the hub's own work on an upgrade, its Access check and its registry read: measured on staging at about 220 to 240 ms (Decision Log 6) and allowed 300 ms. `P` is the handshake's signatures and key agreement on the two ends: allowed 100 ms. `J` is the endpoint's work between the arrival of a `sub` and its `subbed`, for the slowest of the link's subscriptions: its authorization, and for a session's stream the reads `SessionStreams.#open` awaits before it answers (`src/shared/host/stream-catchup.ts`): the status, the `session.events` read that checks the cursor, then the queue, the pending items and the catalog. With `P` at 100 ms the direct carrier fits in 1 s while `6 d + J` is at most 800 ms (`d` at most 133 ms when `J` is nothing), and the relay while `7 h + 3 m + J` is at most 500 ms (50 ms each when `h` and `m` are equal and `J` is nothing). The hub's own work is the largest single share of the relay's second. M4's 7e records `d`, `h` and `m` as §K measures them, and `J` as the machine's own line gives it.

**`J` is not bounded by design today, and grows with the launch's history.** For a cursor the engine host's ring still holds (its last 5,000 events, `RING_SIZE`, `src/engine-host/session-stream.ts`), the read that checks it is from memory and the whole opening took about 0.07 ms. For a cursor the ring has evicted, `SessionStream.fromJournal` reads the launch's journal from its first row through the cursor and one page beyond, through a fresh normaliser, since a read that does not continue the page before it starts over. An hour asleep with the session working is that case. The architect's bench of 2026-11-10 measured it (master caaef871c; an M1 Max under a 1-minute load of about 21; warm file cache; rows of about 430 bytes shaped from the probe fixtures; raw numbers and the bench under `~/.local/share/maws-architect/o15-evidence/resume-cost/`): about 35 ms for each 10,000 journal rows before the cursor (33 to 37). The whole opening for a cursor just evicted took 16 ms on a journal of 10,000 rows, 157 ms at 50,000, 670 ms at 200,000 and 1,714 ms at 500,000; a cursor near the journal's start cost 3 to 5 ms at any size, so the newest evicted cursor is the worst, and a second page after the first about 2 ms. At 500,000 rows the time went to `JSON.parse` (32 %), the line reader (24 %), the normaliser (24 %) and schema validation (5 %). The figure leaves out the authorization, the endpoint's call to the engine host, the sealing and the network. With it, on that machine and load:

| Carrier | Round trips | `J` may be | Journal rows before the cursor |
|---|---|---|---|
| direct | `d` 20 ms | 680 ms | about 190,000 |
| direct | `d` 40 ms | 560 ms | about 160,000 |
| relay | `h` and `m` 20 ms | 300 ms | about 85,000 |
| relay | `h` and `m` 30 ms | 200 ms | about 57,000 |

Past those sizes a stream's `subbed` is later than 1 s whatever the link does. The largest lane B journal on the MacBook's production profile today is 23.5 MiB, about 55,000 rows, which is a `J` of about 0.2 s for a cursor near its end. So the document claims the 1 s only inside this table, and §I's case holds it only for what it runs: two streams whose cursors are really evicted, on journals of about 6,500 and 13,000 events, where `J` is a few tens of ms. The design that would bound `J` is §L's checkpoints: Task 8, built in M2b if the owner rules so (Decision Log 8), otherwise a board ticket. `J` is observable either way: the machine's line for each `sub` carries the milliseconds from the `sub`'s receipt to its `subbed`, and the engine host's journal line the rows it read (§H), so M4's 7e records `J` for each stream on the real machines and the gate case records it for its evicted cursors.

*With Task 8 built* (Decision Logs 27 and 28). A cursor the ring has evicted is now read from the newest checkpoint at or before it, so for a cursor in the dense region (the newest 240,000 rows of a launch) the read takes at most one interval of rows, 10,000, plus the page, whatever the launch's length: measured at 9,302 rows in 79 ms on a journal of 200,000 rows where the full re-read took 199,302 rows and 1,408 ms, under a load of about 19. `J` is then bounded by design inside the dense region, at a few tens of ms on the bench machine, and the third condition reads the table above only for a cursor outside it (older than the dense region, past the byte cap's oldest checkpoint, or in a file the launch no longer holds), where today's scan still runs. The `host.log` line says which, by its `, from the checkpoint at seq <n>` suffix; 7e records it beside `J`.

*Lane A.* The measurement is of lane B's stream. A lane A stream's `J` is bounded: `PlaneA.open` (`src/endpoint/plane-a.ts`) answers `subbed` in the turn it runs, with no read before it. It answers `replay` from an in-memory ring of 2 MiB when the device's own upstream still lives and holds the next message, and otherwise opens a fresh upstream, sends sminos its `subscribe` and answers `snapshot` at once (`#fresh`), before sminos has sent anything. Neither side of MAWS reads a transcript from its start: the app's watch subscribes with no cursor of its own and takes what the link held (`subscribe` in `src/main/lane-a/remote.ts`), and treats any answer but `replay` as a reset. Two things follow for the hour asleep. A dropped channel's upstream is kept for the resume window, 120 s (`RESUME_MS`), so after any sleep longer than that a lane A stream's answer is `snapshot`, never `replay`. And that `subbed` says only that the endpoint has asked sminos: the session's content arrives with sminos's snapshot, whose time is sminos's and is neither part of `J` nor bounded by anything here. M2b's reading counts the stream as resumed at `subbed`; 7e records, for a lane A stream, that it was a snapshot.

Unit (`link.test.ts`, with a fake reach whose answers the test gives, fake carriers and the fake clock it has; times are asserted on that clock). *Today:* with `NO_REACH` every existing case passes unchanged; a loss for `timeout` closes the cached relay socket with no window open; outside a window there is never more than one attempt. *Opening:* a wake of 10 s with a channel closes it for `wake`, closes the relay socket, aborts a hanging attempt, clears the retry timer and starts none of today's attempts; a wake of 9 s with a channel sends today's ping and drops nothing; `probe('network')` with no channel aborts the attempt in flight and enters *lanes*; a second wake in *lanes* aborts both lane attempts and starts both counts and the floor's timer again; a second wake after the floor aborts today's attempt and returns the link to *lanes*; a `focus` probe in *lanes* starts nothing. *Lanes:* a direct answer makes exactly one dial, to that address, with no race and no record fetch, and takes one token; a hub answer opens a new relay socket; both lanes attempt at once when both answer; an answer during a backoff starts the attempt exactly at the backoff's end, not earlier and not later; six failures back off 100, 250, 500, 1,000 and 2,000 ms and then the lane is spent and dials no more though its target answers; with one token in the bucket the direct lane is spent without a dial; both lanes spent fire the floor at once; the cached record is used with no fetch, and a newer one from the background read adds its addresses; a recheck that comes due while the direct lane runs dials nothing and arms its timer again; a lane attempt begun under an older trust generation is discarded and counts as a failure. *The two orders:* both answer and the relay's open stalls: the direct lane adopts within its own steps, the relay attempt is aborted and its socket closed; both answer and the direct handshake stalls: the relay is adopted, the direct attempt ends at its 2 s deadline, the lane tries again and its success is the switch; both complete, relay first: the relay is adopted and then replaced, and one channel and no socket is left; both complete, direct first: the relay's channel and socket are closed; both complete in one turn, in each order; an aborted attempt that completes late adopts nothing and leaves no socket. *Catch-up:* inside the window its redials are made under `holds(2)`, and aborted between two pages it dials no more and leaves no carrier open. *The floor:* after 10 s in *lanes* with no channel and no attempt in flight the lanes' timers are cleared and their waits cancelled, and `startAttempt` runs once; a lane attempt in flight at 10 s is not aborted: when it completes it is adopted and `startAttempt` does not run, and when it fails `startAttempt` runs once then, with no backoff before it; a lane attempt begun at 9.9 s over a path inside the budget is adopted within the lane's own steps; from the floor's time no lane attempt starts though a target that had answered before it answers again; a channel adopted before 10 s clears the timer, and one lost again in *lanes* starts it from zero. *After the floor's time:* a lane whose first answer comes then, on a link with no channel and today's attempt running, aborts that attempt, clears the retry timer and starts its own in the same turn with no second probe, and the channel is adopted within the lane's own steps from that answer; today's attempt aborted so adopts nothing if it completes later, and leaves no socket; the other carrier first answers after the floor's time while the first carrier's attempt is still running to its end: its lane attempts at once, the running attempt is not aborted, `startAttempt` has not run, and the channel is adopted within the lane's own steps from that answer; the floor's timer runs again from the return, and a second floor starts `startAttempt` once more; a lane that had its answer before the floor brings no return when a target of its answers again, or another of its targets for the first time; the second lane's first answer returns the link once more, and nothing returns it a third time; the direct lane's first answer with one token in the bucket aborts nothing and writes its `spent` line; a lane's first answer on a link with a channel starts nothing; a new opening after any of these starts the link over. *Closing:* the window closes with a lane attempt in flight: no retry of today's is scheduled while it runs, it is adopted if it completes, and `scheduleRetry` runs when it fails; the window closes after the floor: today's loop goes on and no answer is waited for. *A recheck in flight:* the relay channel is lost in a window while a recheck's direct dial is running: the relay lane starts, no second direct dial is made, the recheck's channel is adopted when it completes, and its failure counts as the direct lane's. *Acts:* a channel adopted with an act still inside its 60 s, or with queries for older ones, writes the `wrote … ahead of the subscriptions` line once, with the count and the bytes of all of them, before the `sub`s; with nothing to send it writes none.

### §G. The org feed and the project socket (`src/main/org/registry-feed.ts`, `src/main/org/channel/`)

    // WebSocketLike gains (the `ws` client's own: its 'pong' event, ping() and terminate())
    on(event: 'pong', listener: () => void): void;
    ping(): void;
    terminate(): void;
    // OrgTiming gains
    HUB_PING_MS: number;          // 15_000
    HUB_PONG_MS: number;          // 5_000
    HUB_WINDOW_OPEN_MS: number;   // 2_000: an open made while the window is open (wsSocket's 10 s otherwise)
    // createRegistryFeed's, openProjectSocket's and createChannels' deps gain (createOrgSession passes them to its channels)
    wake?: WakeSource;     // none: no wake is heard
    reach?: Reach;         // NO_REACH by default

Both sockets follow the same five rules. "Dropped" means: terminated, forgotten and counted as ended in the same turn (the feed tells its `onClose` listeners; the project socket tells `onClosed(1006)`, so the tab reads as catching up), with nothing waited for from the old socket.

1. *A wake.* At `WAKE_DROP_MS` or more: the window is opened, the socket is dropped if there is one, and the socket is reopened by rule 3 with its backoff at the first step. Under it: a socket that is open is pinged now (rule 2's deadline); with none, the reopen is tried now. A parked feed, and a project socket ended by a terminal code, stay as they are.
2. *Liveness.* From a socket's `open`, a `ping()` every `HUB_PING_MS`. A ping with no `pong` within `HUB_PONG_MS` drops the socket and reopens it by rule 3. Only a `pong` answers a ping. 15 s is the period of `mawsd`'s own heartbeat to the hub, and a dead socket is then found within 20 s; 5 s is far above the 8 to 64 ms the pongs took on staging.
3. *Reopening.* One socket at a time, as today. Before an open the socket waits on `reach.reachable(<the hub's URL>)`, for `WINDOW_FLOOR_MS` at most. An open made while the window is open is given `HUB_WINDOW_OPEN_MS`, not `wsSocket`'s 10 s, for the lanes' reason (§F): a hub whose TCP answers and whose upgrade hangs then frees the socket in 2 s. An open that the reach's answer started (`true`: the hub's path answered a probe; `false`: no window is open, or it closed) is today's open, and one that fails takes the usual backoff before the next wait. An open started at the floor, with no answer yet, keeps its wait: an answer that comes before that socket has opened terminates it and opens again at once; and if it ends with nothing from the hub, the next wait starts with no backoff, the floor having paced it. So these sockets are never idle longer than 10 s where today's code would be opening, and a hub that returns late in the window is still met within a probe's interval.
4. *At `unlock-screen`*, heard from the wake source, the feed does what its `probe()` does: with a socket, a ping now; with none, today's reconnect. The project socket has no unlock probe today and gains none: rule 2 covers it.
5. *Its own end.* Every close the app decides (a wake, a missed pong, a session change, `close()`) is a `terminate()`. `wsSocket`'s `close(1000, 'done')` is no longer used for it.

Lines. The feed: `[org-feed] wake after <ms> ms: dropped the socket | no socket`; `[org-feed] no pong within 5000 ms: dropped the socket`; `[org-feed] probe: ping sent | no socket`; today's `[org-feed] connected to <host>`; `[org-feed] listings: last page, <n> records in <pages> pages` at the page that carries `last`. The project socket, under its `[org-channel] <project's first 8>:` prefix: the same wake and pong lines; `welcomed, resuming after <offset>`; `caught up at <offset>`.

*The target.* From the hub's path being reachable, `connected` and `welcomed` come after 100 ms + 4 h + H: about 0.6 s at `h` of 50 ms with `H` at §F's 300 ms (the feed's upgrade alone measured a median of 252 ms on staging, Decision Log 6). The feed's last listings page and the project's `caught-up` follow after the hub's reads and the pages' transfer, which this arithmetic does not bound. The target for an org of the owner's size is 2 s to the last page and to `caught-up`. The gate case holds the feed to it on loopback (§I); 7e records both sockets' times live and holds neither.

Unit (`registry-feed.test.ts`, `project-socket.test.ts`, `channel.test.ts`, fake sockets, a fake reach, the fake clock): a wake of 10 s terminates the open socket in the same turn, tells the end once and opens exactly one socket when the hub answers; no second socket exists while one is opening; a retry waits its backoff and then the reach; a ping every 15 s; a pong within 5 s keeps the socket, none drops it; a wake of 9 s pings and drops nothing; an open in the window that gets no answer ends at 2 s; a socket that has waited 10 s on the reach opens anyway; the hub's answer while that open has not opened terminates it and opens one more; a floor open that ends with nothing from the hub goes back to waiting with no backoff, and one the hub refused takes the usual backoff; `probe()` with a socket pings; the app's own end calls `terminate()` and never `close()`; the feed's `listings` line counts the pages since the open; the project socket sends `resume` after the offset it held; a parked feed hears a wake and opens nothing.

### §H. Main's log file and the lines

    // src/main/log-file.ts
    export const MAIN_LOG_HELD_LINES = 1_000;
    export interface MainLog {
      /** Opens <dir>/main.log, writes the lines held so far with their own times, and writes every later line as it comes. Never throws. False: the file log is off. */
      install(dir: string): boolean;
    }
    /** Wraps the console's log, info, warn and error, once. Until `install`, the newest MAIN_LOG_HELD_LINES lines are held in memory with their times. */
    export function captureMainLog(console: Console, deps?: { maxBytes?: number; now?(): Date; stderr?(text: string): void }): MainLog;
    // src/shared/host/error-words.ts
    export function errorWords(error: unknown): string;

*Two steps, and one owner of the file.* `src/main/app.ts` calls `captureMainLog(console)` at its load, right after `refuseTestLaunchOnDefaultProfile()` and before the build line. From then on each console call goes to the console as before, and its text, formatted as the console formats it (`node:util`'s `format`), is held in memory one line at a time, each with the time it was said; past 1,000 lines the oldest are dropped and counted. `install(<userData>/logs)` is the first statement of the start that `startWithProfileLock` runs, which only the instance that holds the profile lock reaches (`src/main/files/profile-lock.ts`). It writes the held lines to the file with their own times, a line `<n> earlier lines were not held` first when any were dropped, and from then on writes each line as it is said. So the file holds the build line and everything a launch said before it had the lock, a launch that ends at the repair window included, and exactly one process ever writes or rotates a profile's file. A second launch never installs: what it says goes to its own stdout and stderr and nowhere else. The first instance learns of it through `answerSecondLaunch`, which logs nothing today and gains one line: `[main] a second launch (<its build, or an unknown build>) was handed to this instance`. The render host's mode, which `src/main/index.ts` loads another root for, captures nothing.

*The file.* Every line is `<ISO-8601 UTC with ms> <text>`, the form `host.log` and `mawsd.log` have. At 8 MiB (`LOG_ROTATE_BYTES`) the file is renamed to `main.log.1` and a new one begun. The writer is main's own, not `openSupervisorLog`: that one creates its directory outside its guard, leaves an existing directory's mode as it finds it, and stamps a line when it is written, where a held line must carry the time it was said.

*It never throws.* `install` makes the directory with mode 0700 if it is missing; requires it to be a directory and not a symlink; sets it to 0700 when its mode is wider; creates `main.log` with mode 0600, or sets an existing one to 0600. Any of these that fails (the parent cannot be written, a file stands where the directory should be, the directory is another user's so its mode cannot be changed) turns the file log off: stdout and stderr go on exactly as today, the held lines are let go, and one line goes to stderr, `[main] the file log is off: <why>`. A write that fails later drops that line and says so once the same way.

*What the file is, and what counts as evidence.* `main.log` is a private diagnostic file on the owner's disk, under the profile's own modes. It holds whatever main's console holds today, which includes file paths and the text of errors (the quit guard's `could not save <path>: <message>` in `src/main/lifecycle/quit-guard.ts` is one); it is never copied into the repository (constraint 8). The lines of the table below are another matter: each carries ids, modes, positions, counts, carriers, reasons and addresses, and no message's text, title or path. Only they are evidence. §K gives the patterns that pick them out, and M4 copies only matching lines off a machine.

`errorWords` gives an error's `message`; then, when it has one, its `code` in parentheses; then, for an error with inner `errors` (Node's aggregate for a connect that failed on several addresses), each inner error's `code`, `address` and `port`. For example `connect ETIMEDOUT (ETIMEDOUT; ETIMEDOUT 192.0.2.1:443, ENETUNREACH 2001:db8::1:443)`.

| Where | Line |
|---|---|
| the wake source | `[wake] slept <ms> ms (<resume or clock>)` |
| the reach | §E's three |
| a link, under `[machines] <ref>:` | §F's `wake after …`, `probe <reason>: …`, `attempt (<which>): catch-up, dial <n>`, `attempt failed (<which>): …`, `lane <which>: spent (…)`, `floor after <ms> ms: …`, `lanes again: <host>:<port> answered, <ms> ms after the floor` and `wrote <n> act message(s), <bytes> bytes, ahead of the subscriptions`; today's `up over the <carrier> carrier` and `the <carrier> channel was lost (<reason>); reconnecting` |
| a link's subscriptions (`SubTable.receive`, at `subbed`) | `subscription <id> <what>: subbed <mode> at <position>, asked from <cursor>`: a position is `<epoch>:<seq>:<idx or ->`, and the cursor is the one the `sub` on this channel carried, in the same form, or `none` |
| a link's subscriptions (`SubTable.receive`, at the first delivery after a `subbed`) | `subscription <id> <what>: first <event or snapshot> after subbed, at <position>`: once for a subscription on a channel, at the first `ev` delivered after a `replay` answer, or at the first whole snapshot after a `snapshot` or `missing` one. It shows when content resumed; the bound stays on `subbed` |
| the feed and the project socket | §G's |
| the machine, in `mawsd.log` (`src/endpoint/subscriptions.ts`, where `Subscription.subbed` queues the answer) | `endpoint: channel <key> sub <id> <what>: subbed <mode> at <position>, asked from <cursor>, after <ms> ms`, in the same forms. The milliseconds run from the `sub`'s receipt (`Subscriptions.receive`) to this answer's queueing: they are §F's `J` for that subscription |
| `mawsd`'s hub link (`src/endpoint/hub-link.ts`) | today's `endpoint: the hub connection failed: <why>; connecting again in <n> ms`, with `<why>` now `errorWords` of the socket's error |
| the engine host, in `host.log` (`SessionStream.trim`) | `ring evicted <appSessionId> launch <launchId>: events through seq <n> are the journal's now`, at a launch's first eviction only |
| the engine host, in `host.log` (`SessionStream.snapshot`) | `session.events <appSessionId> after <afterSeq or start>: from the journal, <rows> rows read in <ms> ms`, written at the read's end, when the journal serves the read and the read does not continue the page before it (the stream's kept cursor, `through`). With Task 8 built, `, from the checkpoint at seq <n>` follows where one was used, and each checkpoint taken has §L's line |

`<what>` is `session <lane>:<id>`, with ` cards` for that view and ` agent <agentId>` for a subagent's transcript, or `feed <name>` with its session or the project's first 8 characters where it names one.

Unit: `log-file.test.ts` (a held line is written at `install` with the time it was said, in order; 1,001 lines held leave the newest 1,000 and the count line; a line with its time for each console call and each line of a multi-line one after `install`; the console still gets the text, `error` and `warn` too; the rename at the bound; the parent unwritable, a regular file at the directory's path, a symlink there: each leaves the file log off, one stderr line, and nothing thrown; an existing directory of mode 0755 is 0700 afterwards and an existing file 0600; two captures on one profile, one installed: only that one writes and renames the file, the other writes nothing to it); `error-words.test.ts` (a plain error, one with a code, an aggregate with two inner errors); `subs.test.ts` and the endpoint's table test (the line at each `subbed`, with the asked cursor, `none` for a feed; the first-delivery line once for a subscription on a channel, at an event after a replay and at a snapshot after a snapshot, and again on the next channel); `session-stream.test.ts` (the eviction line once for a launch whatever the count of trims, the journal line at a read's first page and not at its next, with the count of rows it read); the endpoint's line carries the time from receipt to answer on a fake clock; the hub link's test (a failed connect's line carries the code and the inner addresses). `src/main/lifecycle.test.ts` and `src/main/sessions/main-wiring.test.ts` load the real root and mock `./log-file` (M2b's "Touches"). E2e: `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s launch asserts that `<userData>/logs/main.log` exists and that its first line is a time followed by the `[main]` build line with this launch's pid; the packaged job runs the same file against the packaged app, which is where a launch with no terminal first has a log.

### §I. The gate case and its seams

*The seam.* `installWakeSeam(env, packaged, wake)` in `src/main/net/wake.ts` sets `globalThis.__maws_wake = { inject(sleptMs: number): void }` when `env.MAWS_E2E` is `1` and the app is not packaged, and returns its removal; otherwise it sets nothing, as `installRemoteBenchSeam` does for `__maws_remote_bench`. A packaged app has no way to be told of a wake that did not happen. A unit test pins both refusals. It is the only seam M2b adds: the reach probes real loopback ports in the scene, and the case reads `<userData>/logs/main.log` through `test/support/main-log.ts`, which parses a line into its time and text.

*The pipe.* `pipeTo` in `e2e/support/remote-scene.ts` gains three states and a record, shown on `aHub` and `aDirect`. `stall()`: every connection open through the pipe is dead from then on, its bytes discarded both ways and neither side closed until the scene stops, and the pipe stops listening, so a new connect is refused. `hold()`: the pipe listens and accepts, and forwards nothing either way, so a TCP connect succeeds and whatever is sent on it goes unanswered: a path that answers TCP and hangs above it. `restore()`: the pipe listens and pipes every connection made from then on; the dead and the held ones stay as they are. `requests()`: for each connection since the scene began, its time and the first line its client sent, or null for one that sent nothing. The dead connections are what a half-open socket is after a sleep. The refused connect stands in for a SYN that nobody answers, which loopback cannot produce; the probe that hangs is `reach.test.ts`'s case.

*The case*, a second test of `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts`, on a scene of its own with `directPipe`. B runs two sessions on the latency stub, both listed. A is launched once for each carrier over one profile, both times with B's address mapped to the direct pipe (`devOrgDir(true)`): for the direct launch the pipe is open; for the relay launch it is on hold from before the launch, so the launch's own direct race times out and the link comes up over the relay.

1. The link is `up` over the carrier and both of B's sessions are open in A's panes. The log's `subbed` lines since the launch give the link's six subscriptions, §F's six: two streams, two checkout feeds, the attention feed and the artifacts feed.
2. Both pipes are stalled. Each of B's sessions then writes 21 turns that A cannot see, about 6,300 events, past the 6,000 at which the ring first trims (`SessionStream.events`), so each stream's cursor in A is behind what B's ring holds. The ring is filled, not shrunk: its size is a dependency that only unit tests pass (`ringSize` in `src/engine-host/ipc.ts`) and no option reaches a spawned engine host, and a real fill makes the case the production ring and the production journal read. What it does not reach is an hour's journal; that is `J` (§F). At the stub's pace this is about 65 s for each launch.
3. `__maws_wake.inject(3_600_000)`. The log shows the wake line and the link's `wake after 3600000 ms: dropped …` line, whatever it names: by then the link's own silence check has lost the channel.
4. After a wait drawn between 1 and 3 s, `restoredAt` is the runner's clock at the instant of the restore. On the direct launch the direct pipe alone is restored. On the relay launch the hub's pipe is restored and in the same turn the direct pipe is put on hold, so the direct lane's target answers TCP and its upgrade hangs.
