30. **2026-11-10, the architect, on Task 8's stop: a launch whose live state may differ from its journal takes no more live checkpoints.** Task 8's review reproduced two answers that differ from the full re-read: an engine frame whose `event.index` is the literal `1e400` parses to `Infinity`, which the journal writes as `null`, and a `-0` the journal writes as `0`; a live checkpoint holds what the live normaliser saw, the rows read on from it are the journal's, and a later delta lost its tool's id and name, a later completion carried `blockIndex -0` against the scan's `0` (Decision Log 27's stop rule; the repro outside the repository). Scan-built checkpoints are unaffected. The pinned engine writes its stream with Node's `JSON.stringify`, which emits neither, but nothing in MAWS enforces it. Taken: the session detects, per engine line, a line whose JSON does not reach the journal unchanged; on the first, that launch takes no more live checkpoints, and its reads fall back to scan-built ones, which match the journal by construction. Rejected: no live checkpoints at all (the first resume after a long sleep, the read checkpoints exist for, would always be a full scan); normalising `-0` and `Infinity` at the transport's parse (it changes what the live stream answers, constraint 1); a JSON.parse reviver (correct, and a cost on every row). Four requirements bind the build. (1) No false positive on ordinary content: `-0`, long digit runs and exponents occur inside strings all the time, and a detector that tripped on them would quietly switch live checkpoints off in most long launches. So it does not look inside JSON strings, by one of two means, chosen by what the code holds and named in §L: (a) the frame's own `JSON.stringify` text, where the journal row holds it, compared byte for byte with the raw engine line, usable only if every line of `spikes/protocol-probe/fixtures/` equals `JSON.stringify(JSON.parse(line))`, which a unit case proves; or (b) one linear string-aware pass that skips string literals and tests number tokens outside them (a negative zero in any form, an exponent that overflows or underflows to another value, a digit run past what a double holds), whose unit cases are the review's two frames tripping it, the same texts inside a string not, and no fixture line tripping it. (2) It is visible: the first time a launch goes off live checkpoints, one `host.log` line says so with the launch and the seq, in a form §K's `extract` copies, and never again for that launch. (3) Its cost is measured, the detector timed over the fixtures' lines in the existing bench test's style, and the per-line figure written into §L beside "What the live path pays"; the measured group's bounds judge it on the Mac. (4) The property test's generator stays as it is, with the review's two frames as named cases held to the full scan's answers, and the live-boundary case of Decision Log 29 (a). Left as it is (constraint 1): for a frame the pinned engine cannot write, a live stream and a later journal replay already disagree on master with no checkpoint; M5 writes the tracker row.
31. **2026-11-10, the architect's ruling at 18:06Z, made before any measured number of the wake case existed: how the case on the final build (F, M3) admits, voids and labels a wake.** The measured run on 4b8f99326 is an early read under the code as it stands; O15's verdict on acceptance 6 comes from the run on F. To be built on the branch, test-only, at the merge of master after p5-o15 lands (it brings the artifacts case's void form, p5-o15 372cd6dd6). (a) Per carrier, `quietWait` admits the launch before the stall, not inside it: after a real sleep the app wakes still believing its channel is up, and today's short stall is the scene that models that, so the stall stays as short as it is, followed as today by the turns, the page-row reads, the inject and the restore. A wait inside the stall would have the link already lost and retrying at the inject; by `open('wake')` the timed result may be the same, but the measured scene would no longer be the real one. (b) The 1-minute load is read per carrier at instants that bracket every timed window: at the inject, after the last `subbed`, and around the feed's window where the hub's restore comes later, so that no timed window lies outside a pair of reads. A read of 5 or more voids the run in the artifacts case's form (`void: true`, `pass: null`, "void by load, not a bound miss"). For this case Rule B's samples and the start and end loads stay printed as context and void nothing; the group's first test keeps Rule B. (c) `conditions` count from `injectedAt`, where §F's window opens; the loss lines before the inject stay in `record.lost`. Counting from the stall would let today's attempts against the refused pipes, in a stall that outlasts the link's silence check (three periods of 5 to 7 s), read as one of §F's conditions. (d) A bound miss while `conditions` is non-empty fails labelled as outside §F's conditions (`outside: true`, `pass: null`): red, so it can read neither as a breach nor as a pass. (e) The record keeps `linkBeforeWake` and gains each carrier's stall length, so a run shows whether the link still held its channel at the inject. What the code on 4b8f99326 does instead, read before the ruling: it voids on Rule B alone (a start or end load of 5 or more voids nothing by itself, against the comment at `e2e/remote-latency.spec.ts` line 574), it holds every bound whatever `conditions` holds, and it counts `conditions` from the stall.
32. **2026-11-10, the architect, on panel 1's two P2 findings (0e1718bbd..82ac18290), built in the fix wave be1f5869e, 40f5e9b47.** (a) *The reach's resolver work is bounded* (§E). Each probe of a hostname called `net.connect({ host, port })`, which starts a `dns.lookup` of its own that the probe's deadline cannot cancel; with a stalled resolver the panel measured 101 lookups outstanding after 10 s and 600 at the window's close, against 30 sockets, on the four-thread pool the real dial's lookup shares. The ruling: a probe starts no lookup, at most one lookup is in flight for a host, an IP literal is connected to as today, a name's answer is probed in both families, a failed lookup or an answer whose addresses all failed is looked up again one at a time, and an answer that comes after its round ended is dropped. The executor bounds the lookup per host across the whole reach rather than per round, since rounds left and rejoined, or spanning two windows, would otherwise each start their own; the re-lookup trigger is a failed probe to each address of the current answer since it came, the simplest reading of "its addresses stop answering". (b) *The clock notice's cooldown survives a backward correction* (§D): a `lastClockNotice` later than the tick counts as none, so a gap after the wall clock is set back is told. (c) *Not changed, recorded:* the real dial's lookup (`src/main/machines/remote/carriers.ts`, the relay and direct opens; `src/main/org/registry-feed.ts`, the feed's open) has the same exposure in milder form, one uncancelled lookup per abandoned attempt, bounded by the lanes' six attempts and their backoff, today's loop and the feed's one open in flight rather than by a 100 ms stagger; it is left as it is, for the architect.
33. **2026-11-10, the architect, on the early measured run (42906030773) and on F's wake case: Decision Log 31 revised.** This entry replaces 31 (a), (b) and (c)'s reading of what counts as a condition, keeps 31's void rule and its label for a miss outside §F's conditions, and adds to the record. Why: 31 (a) rested on the executor's estimate that today's stall lasts about 10 s, so that the link still holds its channel at the inject. The run showed it wrong: the stall lasts as long as B's writing of 21 turns to each session, about 45 s, past the link's silence check of three periods of 5 to 7 s, and both carriers met the inject `down/none` with today's attempts failing for some 42 s. (a) *What the scene models is said as it is.* The link has noticed the loss and is retrying when the wake arrives; `linkBeforeWake` reads `down/none` and stays in the record. "The app wakes believing its channel is up" is not this case's state but 7e's, with a real sleep. No seam goes into product code to hold the silence check, and the stall is not shortened by muting delivery: after `open('wake')` both states run the same path (what exists is dropped, the window opens, the lanes run). So that the other state is not left to 7e alone, it has a unit case: the drop half is `link.test.ts`'s "a wake of 10 s with a channel closes it for `wake`, closes the relay socket, aborts a hanging attempt and starts none of today's"; no unit case yet takes a wake with the channel up through to the next channel, each subscription sent again and a stream's from its cursor, and one is added at the unit level with the build. (b) *The settle replaces 31's "before the stall".* `quietWait` (`e2e/support/quiet.ts`; Decision Log 34 (a)) runs once per carrier after B's writes for that carrier are complete and before the inject, with the pipes still held: the link is down by then whatever the wait, and the timed windows start at a settled load whatever the scene's own setup cost. The stall then runs past B's `RESUME_MS` of 120 s when the settle is slow; read before building, B's ended interest changes nothing the case measures (withdrawn as unproven, Decision Log 37 (c)). A closed channel's report is kept for `RESUME_MS` and then dropped, which only starts the idle release's clock (10 min, `DEFAULT_IDLE_EXIT_MS`); a release would trim the ring and keep the stream and its checkpoints in any case (the stream is disposed only at the session's next launch). The `subbed` answers, the journal reads and the replay from the held cursor do not read the interest. The other `RESUME_MS` uses (lane A's upstream, a pty's input record, the forwards) are not in the scene. (c) *The void rule is 31's (b).* The 1-minute load is read per carrier at the inject, after the last `subbed`, and around the feed's window where the hub's restore is later; 5 or more on any read voids the run in artifacts-perf's form; for this case Rule B's samples and the start and end loads are printed as context only. (d) *`conditions` holds only what §F lists, counted from `injectedAt`* (31 (c) kept for the start). §F's fourth condition is about the lane that was adopted, so an `attempt failed` line of the other lane (the direct lane on the held pipe in the relay's launch, `+1052 ms … did not open within 1000 ms` in the early run) is not a condition; such lines stay in the record under a name of their own. A bound miss while `conditions` is non-empty fails labelled as outside §F's conditions, as 31 (d) said. (e) *The record* keeps `linkBeforeWake` and gains, per carrier, the stall's length and the settle's. (f) *The order.* Built test-only on the merge of master after p5-o15 lands; then class 1a; then one run of `e2e:remote-latency` alone on the merged head as the revised case's first Mac run, on the architect's word and CI-opt's GO, before any `--gates all`. The early run stays void, with no rerun: the load rose from 4.13 to 6.71 on a held mini with nothing else running, so the case's own work raises it, and the same code would most likely void the same way.
34. **2026-11-10, M2b's executor, building Decision Log 33 (5fee8f36a, 4bdad1036, 337940ffa): what 33 left open.** (a) `quietWait` is `e2e/support/quiet.ts` (only its test is under `test/support/`); 31 and 33 named the test's directory. (b) *The load reads*, each `loadavg()[0]` rounded to two places as `quiet.ts` rounds: at the inject, just before `injectedAt`; right after the wait for every `subbed` returns, taken there even when the wait timed out; on the direct launch, just before the hub's restore; and after a bounded wait for the feed's last-page line from the hub's restore (on the relay launch it usually returns at once). The reads are kept per carrier even when that launch's evidence is unavailable, so they can still void the run. The void message names, per carrier, each read at 5 or more and lists all of them. (c) *The lane adopted* is the carrier of the first `up` line after the restore, the case's carrier where there is none. An `attempt failed` or a `lane …: spent` line naming the other of direct and relay goes to `otherLane`; today's `attempt failed`, `catch-up` (whatever lane it names), `floor` and `acts-ahead` are always conditions. (d) `beforeInject` keeps the condition-kind lines from the stall to the inject (today's attempts against the refused pipes) as context, so the evidence that the link met the wake already retrying stays in the record; `lost` is unchanged. Times stay relative to the restore throughout, so lines between the inject and the restore read negative. (e) *The verdict*: `void` when any window read is 5 or more; misses split per carrier, a carrier with `conditions` giving outside misses and one without plain misses; `pass` null when void, false with any failure or plain miss, null with only outside misses, true otherwise, so a plain miss on one carrier beside an outside miss on the other is `pass: false` with `outside: true`. The `expect`s run void, failures, plain misses, outside misses, in that order; the check mode holds only `failures`. `remote-wake.json`'s top level carries `void`, `outside`, `pass`, `voidReasons`, `boundMisses`, `outsideMisses` and `outsideConditions`, with Rule B under `load.ruleB` as context; `valid` and `invalidReasons` are gone. (f) `linkBeforeWake` is read after the settle, just before the inject: the link as the wake finds it. (g) The case's own timeout is 26 minutes (`test.setTimeout`), for the two settles; the group's 60 minutes are unchanged. (h) Under this rule the early run's records would both have empty `conditions`: its `attempt failed (today's)` lines fall before the inject, and `+1052 ms attempt failed (direct)` is the relay launch's `otherLane`. (i) The unit case of 33 (a) is `link.test.ts`'s "a wake of an hour with the channel up: the next channel adopted, each subscription sent again on it, the stream's from exactly its held cursor"; it passes on the code as it was, pinning the behaviour.

40. **2026-11-11, M2b's executor with the architect, building 39 (a) (7ef254c50, 5629b9677, 159dccc4f, db624f987, 080ce0d65, f8f1d41b0; 76ccfafec built and reverted): the trigger, the design the architect approved, and what the build decided.** (a) *The trigger is the tab, not the roster.* Diagnostic run 42927485275 logged no record dropped and B stayed in `listedMachines`; each pane's transcript view unmounted about 22 s into the stall, at the link's loss, and mounted again at `up`, then hydrated a kept key with no holder from a whole `session.events` read. `SessionTab` (`src/renderer/src/main/Pane.tsx`) draws P5 Decision Log 28 T5's "<B> is offline" in place of `SessionPane` whenever the machine is not `up`; the unmount detached the transcript store's last holder, and `attach` starts such a key over from the whole history. T5 serves exactly "the link was down" and the store keeps its rows, so neither of 39 (b)'s stop conditions held; the architect approved the design before it was built. (b) *The hold.* While a remote lane B tab is mounted it holds its session's transcript key (`src/renderer/src/lane-b/transcript-hold.ts`, `useRemoteTranscriptHold`), the same store key and memoised `ipcSource` the pane uses, so the pane's attach at `up` joins the held attachment and a `replay` opening delivers into the subscription that lived through the loss. It keeps a healthy attachment and never makes one: it joins only once the roster names the machine `up` and the pane has attached, so a tab restored or opened while its machine is offline (or before the roster is read) holds nothing and hydrates at `up` as before. A held key that is not whole at the return (its hydration failed or still out) is read again once, at the stream's return (`remote.session` past `paused` and `opening`). Local and socket-linked sessions are unchanged (no cursor replay and no `remote.gap` there; a hold would leave a silent hole). (c) *The armed gap.* The session source arms at the roster's loss and disarms at the first batch after the return; a `remote.gap` while armed starts the store's staged rebuild (`pager.refresh()`), old rows drawn until the whole history is in, its bar carried into the new subscription's held list so the snapshot places it as a hydration does. A gap heard before the stream is back (a history read's left-out row, `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts` ~694, is its second emitter) is held until `remote.session` said since the loss is past `paused` and `opening`, so no re-read starts before that stream's `subbed`; a state said before the loss does not count. An unarmed gap draws only its bar, as before. (d) *The source across a loss*, read against main: at an RL2 loss main sends `remote.session` (`paused`), `machines.changed` and no `engine.hostState` (main emits that for its own supervisor only), so `onHostLoss` never runs; no `session.changed` or `fs.followState` comes; at the return `session.changed` resets only for a custody change and the `stale` refresh cannot fire. The held source neither resets, nor switches to disk, nor reads its history at a plain loss; a unit case pins it. (e) *The wake case's proof.* B's logs already name both events, so no line was added: the whole-launch read is B's `session.events <app> after start: from the journal` line, and the stream's answer B's endpoint `subbed` line (A's where B's is missing); `readWake` fails a session whose read's span starts before that answer and ends after the restore, in both carriers, folded into `wakeVerdict` as the other structural failures. A whole read B answers from its ring writes no line; in this scene the ring has trimmed past the launch's start. (f) *Left as they are:* a failed key meeting a `snapshot` opening re-reads without the bar (the re-read is whole, so nothing is missing); a hydration read in flight at a short loss can land after the return, since RL2 calls that are not `oneChannel` wait out the call timeout, as before. The transcript's scroll offset, reset at every reconnect by the remount T5 keeps, is a separate defect the architect registers. (g) *The return's first word decides* (`reviewer-high`'s P1, fixed in db624f987). A daemon of B restarted during the loss without the session's live launch ends the paused subscription `unavailable`; main says `idle` with no gap and no batch, and a key held `live` from before the loss would keep a hole for good where the remount had read B's disk. So a held key keeps its rows unread only when the stream's first `remote.session` word after the loss is `live` (the paused subscription resumed by replay); `gap` goes through the armed gap; `idle` or `ended` start one staged re-read at that word, any held gap joined into it. A fresh `#subscribe` (whose `snapshot` opening would say `live` with no gap) is not reachable before that first word: a plain loss keeps the same subscription (`subs.ts` `onOffline` pauses it, `onCurrent` sends its `sub` again to the same sink), every end of a paused subscription says `ended` or `idle` first, and the silent `behind` re-subscription comes only after a `subbed` has been sent. `remote.session` carries no subscription identity, so this rests on that ordering in main and on B, not on a renderer check. (h) *Main says the answer; the renderer no longer infers it* (`reviewer-high`'s second round, two P2s, fixed in 080ce0d65 under the architect's conditions; this replaces (c)'s arming and decision and (g)'s rule). The second round refuted (g)'s last sentence twice: a backed-off retry of an unavailable stream firing during the loss makes a subscription that the link's republish of `idle` at the return passed off as an answer, so the whole read started before `subbed`; and a `behind` re-subscription cut by the loss keeps a fresh sink (`first` true) whose `snapshot` answer tells no gap and says `live`, which kept the old launch's rows under the new one's. `remote.session` now carries `answer: {n, mode} | null` (`src/shared/ipc/schema/remote.ts`; additive and optional, an IPC member only, `wire-manifest.json` unchanged, since the `remote` namespace never crosses a link). `n` rises at each `subbed` with its mode (`replay`, `missing`, `snapshot`), whichever sink answered, and at an end B sends while the link is `up` (`unavailable`, or `ended` for any other reason but `behind`); a republish for a link or caps change leaves it, and the adapter (`src/main/lane-b/remote.ts`) keeps one count per machine and session, so a record made again goes on from it. What a subscription end sets: every end flushes, tells a pending gap, keeps the cursor and clears the subscription; `removed` and `closed` (the link's own ends: refused, closed, a new link object) stop the record without touching the answer; `behind` reopens at once without touching it; only `unavailable` and B's other ends move it, and only while the link is `up`. A loss cannot make an answer: a plain loss only pauses its subscription (`subs.ts` `onOffline`), the loss's own ends happen with the link not `up`, and B's ends come only over a current channel, so the first `n` past the one held at the loss answers a subscription made after the return. The source and the tab's hold arm on main's own `remote.session` saying the link `down` after `up`, taking the `n` it carries: main says it before the roster in the same step and in order with its answers, so an answer main receives before the renderer's roster hears of the loss still decides. A subscription or hold that first hears the link already `down` has nothing armed. The first answer past the armed `n` decides: `replay` keeps the rows and reads nothing (gaps held meanwhile draw their bars); `missing`, `snapshot`, `unavailable` and `ended` start one staged re-read carrying them; nothing is read before it. The hold's retry of a key not whole at the return, a failed one included, waits for the same answer. A link that only reconnects within its grace never arms: `reconnecting` is reported `up` (`src/main/machines/remote/link.ts` `REPORTED`), the roster stays `up` and the pane never unmounts, so a `snapshot` or `missing` answer there is handled as on master. (i) *A return after which nothing subscribes: built, taken out, left open* (the architect). An `idle` stream the checkout feed follows is answered after a return (the feed's `subbed` on the new channel calls `following`, which is `kick()`, `src/main/lane-b/remote.ts` ~320, and the try ends `unavailable` with the link up). Nothing subscribes after the return for a stream `ended` at the loss (`kick` returns for it), or one waiting on its probe of up to 30 minutes (a peer without `rl2.feeds`, a feed that does not open again); there a held key that was failed or still hydrating is not read again by itself, and gaps heard while armed are drawn late, until the stream's next answer. 76ccfafec built a repair, main answering `none` at the link's `down` then `up` for a record with no subscription outstanding; its scoped re-review reproduced a P2, the known trap for whoever repairs it: `#sub` is also null while `#readRecord` is opening the session (a session reopened after main discarded its lane, rendered from the renderer's cached record), so `none` authorised the whole read and the opening's subscription then overlapped it. Fixing that meant answers at every exit of the record ladder, more new behaviour in main than the hole is worth on the night of the final build, so the architect took the round out (reverted) and left the hole open; the branch goes to the final build on 080ce0d65's mechanism, whose re-review was clean. The repair's direction: main answers a return that no subscription and no opening follows. Open, not built: a tab whose first word after mounting is `down` (a cold mount) holds nothing and its pane hydrates at roster `up` before that stream's `subbed`, as on master; a visible tab of an idle remote session reads its whole launch at every return, as on master, which can run on the other machine's engine host beside the live streams' resumes (the wake case has no such tab). Rows stay single across a staged re-read and a replay of the same ids (`source/hydrate.ts`, `stores/transcript.ts` `seen`); a disk-form against live-form overlap is the existing hydrate-while-live path, not this change's. (j) *The architect's review panel on eb001d02b..411b76c4a* (eight agents; three findings confirmed, all from the test-quality lens; three refuted: that the wake case should see whole-launch requests independently of B's journal line, the documented limit of (e), and two on the hold's view of the store's attachment insertion, whose consequence is the first finding). Fixed in f8f1d41b0, each test failing first, a scoped `reviewer-medium` review clean: the store publishes `hydrating` before it counts the pane's attachment, so a hold installed with the pane acquires at the next notification, and when that was main's word of the loss (which comes before the roster's) `check()` acquired and returned before arming, so a hydration that failed during the outage stayed failed after a `replay` return; the acquiring call now goes on to arm, and every loss test is in main's order (the session's word, then the roster). An adapter test pins that a record made again for a new link object continues the session's answer count, and the local source's case asserts the `remote.session` listener this change adds.

A network loss with the Mac awake, 08:37:41Z to about 09:41:57Z: both links were up over the direct carrier 0.6 to 1.2 s after their targets answered again; the probe's connects to the hub failed until 09:42:26.9Z, 28.6 s after the app's own socket to the same host connected at 09:41:58.3Z, which is Decision Log 6 (k)'s risk seen once; and main's `[reach] window open for 60 s (network)` came about 27 s after the links were up. (g) *Findings, each carried with a tracker row and put to the owner at M5; nothing is waived.* (1) The repair window, (c). (2) The MacBook's idle footprint, (d). (3) `src/endpoint/tailnet.ts:37-39` counts any 100.64/10 IPv4 as tailnet; the owner's Wi-Fi hands out 100.112.64.0/18 from that range, so `mawsd` listens on the Wi-Fi LAN on both Macs and the MacBook's link to the mini runs over the LAN labelled "direct (tailnet)": rows 15 and 4q read so. The fix's direction is to find the tailnet by its interface, not by address range. (4) After the MacBook's swap, a timed-out `engine.configure` push was kept and refused every local lane B start until an engine-host restart at 01:39:42Z (`src/services/settings/engine-configure.ts:64-86, 120-133`); the cause stays in F. (5) A Stop whose interrupt fails shows only a `console.warn` (`src/renderer/src/composer/Composer.tsx:399`), and no e2e presses Stop on a remote session. (6) The mini's own window shows no session of its 4-mini project, though the index holds them. (7) 3b: `sminos list --org` publishes another machine's state at 5 s ticks after two agreeing ticks, so it lags 5 to 10 s by design and the 2 s rule against it fails by construction; and the row of a remote lane B session with its tab open never drew Working (the desk track's analysis is in the evidence, `part-a/3b/`). (8) 2b: the mini's window drew the MacBook's session "Needs approval" while sminos read it busy during the quit. (9) 15 with the app quit: the engine's loopback listener 127.0.0.1:61966, for the owner to judge. (10) While MAWS's window is off screen its renderer lags badly (the palette about a minute late; the project chooser never opened). (11) The Channel tab read "reading channel" for about 45 s during the network loss (the owner's own count about 40 s) and never an offline state. (12) The bench cannot run from this MacBook under Rule B; the tracker's live-bench row now names the mini as the runner (`o15-evidence/bench-readiness.md` §B) or the owner's ruling. (13) The late `[reach]` line of (f). (14) MAWS's unit tests leak their fake `gcloud` as a polling orphan (`maws-org-session-*`, `maws-delegations-*`; two found on the MacBook about three days old). (h) *Observations, no row:* a remote lane B session cannot be stopped from another machine's MAWS (`session.stop` is not an act its link carries); on the MacBook's local S-2a an answered approval card stayed in the composer through a 60 s tool run; 4l's render-host line names `artifact_export` for an artifact's screenshot, which is a PNG export marked inspection by design (`src/engine-host/tool-server/artifact-inspection.ts:10-28`); a `session.stop` of the mini's row 7 session, with its question pending, returned code 1; the MacBook's quit asked no question, as M2 made it; the step-up sheet for the VM's folder named the machine by its ref, not by its name. (i) *What stays open.* M5, with the verdict asked from 2026-11-14. Row 7 and 4m to 4p have no date and keep their setups. At the verdict the owner judges 3b's reference, 15's engine listener and LAN bind, 4q's mini half and the repair window, with the footprint beside them. P5's Decision Log 271.
46. **2026-11-13, the architect, at M5's record: P5's Outcomes holds the 67 results and the rest of what M5 asks, the verdict is pending, and five places of M5's text are corrected by the sources.** (a) *The record.* P5's "Outcomes and retrospective" is written from this document's Progress and its Decision Logs 44 and 45, P5's Decision Logs 266 to 273, the two inventories and the evidence folders' results files: the table of the 67 results, each with its date, build and evidence; what each child delivered; the items handed to O15 by name; what is carried, each with its tracker row or board ticket; the lessons. The verdict's line reads "Verdict: asked from 2026-11-14 (O15 M5); recorded here in the owner's words." Nothing of M4 is re-decided: 7a to 7e stay not run, 4m to 4p refused, 14a and 14b void, and the findings stay the owner's to judge. (b) *M5's Decisions, done.* The Risks row is corrected and not closed, and a tracker row carries the keys' custody. The tracker rows M5 names are written, with the residue of M1, M2 and M2b and of the closing run that had no row yet: 21 rows and three amendments, listed in P5's Decision Log 274. P5 §2 has its dated sentence. The registry's Progress records F. (c) *The table's claim ids are the inventory's.* The sitting's cells for 4a to 4l follow the runbook's twelve actions (approve and deny apart, the artifact's open, edit and screenshot together), and Progress's line uses those letters ("4k printed `Darwin` and `Linux`", "4l's screenshots"). In the table the inventory's 4e holds the approve and the deny cells, 4j the terminal, 4k the artifact's open and edit, and 4l its screenshot. For 15a to 15c the table repeats the row's one verdict on each claim and says which reading bears on which. (d) *Corrected by the sources.* (1) A lost range is drawn. M2b's decisions and M5's bullet say the renderer shows no gap on a resume that lost a range (tracker row #407), but since O7 M2 the transcript draws "Earlier history isn't available" for a `remote.gap` and for a page answered `historyUnavailable`, pinned by unit cases; 2c's read in Part A looked for that very bar. The row is amended as met and left for the architect's triage; what is open at a link's return is board 565's. (2) O14 B2's step 9 was fixed by PR #343 (P5 Decision Log 136); #345, which M5 names, fixed step 7. That its logs are past retention is M5's own statement, and no other record here says so. (3) O4's Keychain adoption is not closed by 1d's row: the Connectors read was not taken (Progress, 1b to 1d). It is carried with a tracker row. (4) The sminos change M5 expects for the owner label had shipped on 2026-11-10 as sminos 1.43.1, and the three machines ran 1.46.0 at F; the row records the version and what an older frame can still do. (5) The MacBook's cold idle reading is not closed either: 14a and 14b are void, and as read about 8 times the footprint bound. (e) *What stays open.* The verdict, and with it Decision Log 45 (i)'s judgments; row 7 and the bench, with no date; M5's box, ticked with the verdict's commit. O15 is not marked done before it. P5's Decision Log 274.
47. **2026-11-14, the architect, at M5's end: the owner's verdict is given, and O15 is done.** P5: "완료, 예외는 이월 (Recommended)"; 16b: "선언함"; 16c: "없었음"; O14 B2's step 9: "닫기 (Recommended)". The exceptions of Decision Log 45 (i) are carried, none waived or re-decided. P5's Decision Log 276 has the questions and the answers.

## Outcomes & Retrospective

**M1 and M2, 2026-11-10 (the executor).** Both are built on `p5-o15` and reviewed: each at its task review, with fixes re-reviewed, and the whole branch at `doperpowers:reviewer-high`, which found no material defect (Decision Log 1). A multi-lens panel the architect then ran found three small defects that none of those single reviewers had found, all on the repair path or its tests, and they were fixed before landing (Decision Log 16). On a change whose failure path is all the owner sees, the panel was worth its cost. M2b and M3 to M5 are outside this branch.

*What holds on the branch's head.* Main composes none of the machine's services. The main profile's launch has two outcomes, `mawsd` or the repair window, and nothing on the way to the window throws past it. A profile saved on the embedded shape opens on `mawsd` with its settings kept, at client settings version 21. Acceptance 3's data has a cross-build scene of its own. No suite launches the embedded shape. No wire member was narrowed and no stored version moved. The suites' evidence is in the report (`2026-11-10-p5-o15-report.md`, "Validation").

*What it cost against the plan.* The plan put the risk in the deletion. It was in the proof, and in the measuring:
- M2's code passed its review with two findings, both on the repair path, which is the only thing the owner sees when a launch fails. The executor's walk of that path found two more uncaught calls, and the next review round found a fifth.
- The acceptance-3 scene needed a canary before it proved a reconciliation rather than a cached read, and a missing `turn 1` before it ran at all. Both came from a scene written without a Mac run of its own. A new e2e scene's first Mac run is part of its writing, not of the final gate.
- The ack bound took most of the day. M1's port of `e2e:artifacts-perf` to `mawsd` measured the daemon shape's real cost per acknowledged edit (about 0.7 ms at p50 and 1.1 ms at p95) on a bound that the utility shape had already drifted to within half a millisecond of. It also showed that the case loaded its own windows: three of eight runs that day were void by the load of the case's own launches. The owner re-derived the bound at 13 ms. The case now admits every measured window and voids a trial or a window measured in load.
- The lesson for the next port of a measured group is to read the bound's margin on the old shape the same day before porting, and to admit every window, not only the first.

*Process.* Mac time was cleared window by window with the architect. One red result reached the architect two hours late, because three gates were chained in one background command; each gate now runs in its own command. One fix was applied before the architect had lifted a no-change instruction; it was the confirmed fix, and the rule now is to ask first.

**M2b, 2026-11-11 (M2b's executor).** Built on `p5-o15-wake`: Tasks 1 to 9 (Task 8 ruled in by Decision Log 27), the gate's wake case and its admission rules (Decision Logs 31, 33, 36 to 38), and the fix the case's own measurement called for (Decision Logs 39 to 41). Each task was reviewed against its brief, the whole branch at `reviewer-high`, two of the architect's panels on later ranges, and fix (a) through four review rounds and a scoped re-review of the panel's fix wave.

*What holds on the code head f8f1d41b0.* main's log is on disk under the profile, with the error words and host.log's ring and journal lines (§H, §J); the wake source and the reach (§D, §E); the link through a wake on its two lanes with the floor (§F); the org feed and the project socket after a wake (§G); checkpoints in the session stream with the numbers detector (§L); the 7e probe (§K). A remote session's open tab keeps its transcript across a link loss: the tab holds its key through the offline placeholder, main says each stream's answer (`answer {n, mode}` on `remote.session`), and only a `replay` answer keeps the rows without a read; anything else reads once, staged, never before that stream's `subbed`. On the mini (run 42948213134) the wake case passes on both carriers at valid load, every subscription within 1 s of the path's return (direct 200 ms, relay 457 ms), with no whole-launch read on B; four of the five other measured groups are within their bounds. The render group is read case by case and has no verdict at the PR: :359 one valid reading within (99,107 KiB) and one void; :449 no valid reading (97,330 and 95,490, both void); :479 valid at 101,443 and 100,802, over the single-session bound as constructed; the architect runs it again and records the verdict when the final build is named (Decision Log 42 (h)).

*What it cost against the plan.* The plan put M2b's risk in the network paths (§D to §G), which took one fix wave each. It was in the measurement. The wake case's first valid green read showed a `J` past the plan's estimate; reading B's own lines in the defined state, then timing B's steps and A's calls on a branch that was never merged, placed it in A's panes reading their whole history again inside B's answer, which no spec section had foreseen: the tab's offline placeholder (P5 T5) unmounted the pane at each loss. The fix took four review rounds, each finding a case where the renderer inferred from a published state what only main knew; the repair that held was to have main say the fact (`answer`), and one further round (`none`) was built and taken out as worth less than its risk on the night (Decision Log 40 (i)). Two lessons: a measured number that misses its estimate is read in the defined state on both machines before anything is changed; and where a client must decide on another process's progress, the deciding fact is sent, not reconstructed from state.

*Process.* Gates ran off the mini (class 1a, hosted checks, list A on GitHub's runners) except the measured set, which ran once on the architect's word and CI-opt's GO. Hosted macOS runners read loads of 7 to 19 on the direct window and voided every wake check there; only the relay windows were valid, so hosted checks proved structure and the mini proved the numbers. Three flakes on files the branch does not touch (`recovery.test.ts:656`, fixed on master; `latency-engine-stub.test.ts:136`; `document-perf.test.ts:122`) each cost one rerun.

**M3 to M5, 2026-11-13 (the architect), before the verdict.** The three machines and production's hub run one build, F (master f1852de3a), the closing run ran on it, and P5's Outcomes holds its 67 results (Decision Logs 44 to 46).

*What holds against this document's acceptance.* 1, one arrangement: shown by the suites as its text says (M2), and in both live reads of the MacBook's processes (Part A's start, the repair window's check) no engine host or pty host was a child of the app. 2, the repair window: held by M2's cases in the suites, and a finding live, since a launch with MAWS off under Login Items bootstrapped the unit and drew no window (Decision Log 45 (c)). 3, nothing lost: the cross-build scene on F's code (run 42968927884), and the counts and the index file across the swap on both Macs. 4, the acceptance as a whole: each of the 67 claims has its line in P5's Outcomes. 46 pass and one is cited; the others are findings, void, refused or not run under the owner's rulings, each with a tracker row, and the findings wait for the owner's judgment, so this item is open until the verdict. 5, the record: written but for the owner's words. 6, resume after a wake: held in the gate on the mini over both carriers (run 42948213134), with main's log on disk and read all through M4; its live reading, 7e, is not run.

*What it cost against the plan.* The plan put the owner's time at three sittings and an hour asleep. Part A ran from 2026-11-11 22:20Z to 2026-11-12 02:02Z against about 2 h 10 min planned: the MacBook locked itself for 57 minutes, and a lane B start was refused until an engine-host restart. Part B ran from 04:05Z to 05:40Z against about 70 minutes, the quiet window from 05:47Z to 07:24Z against about 45, and the asleep hour was set up and not run. The measured checks cost the most and returned the least. The render group took eight runs on the mini to decide one case, over by 441 to 793 KiB. Both checks planned from the MacBook, the bench and 14a with 14b, have no valid reading, since that Mac read loads of 19 to 108 in every reading of the week. The owner worked on the MacBook through the sittings, so the drivers entered text by AX value with MAWS in the background. Three faults were the instrument's and are recorded as that: 3b's first loop lost its row, 4d's first presses landed on an unfocused window, and 4q's card to the owner framed the mini's path as a second tailnet address.

*Process.* The record follows the sources where they and M5's text differ (Decision Log 46 (d)). The lessons are in P5's Outcomes; the two that would change the next closing run are that a check which needs the owner's machine asleep or idle goes first, and that a measured row names a runner nobody works on.

**The verdict, 2026-11-14 (the architect).** The owner gave it in two questions after 08:00Z: P5 done with its exceptions carried, MAWS declared the one interface, and nothing in the week that sent them to a terminal or to desk-web (Decision Log 47; P5 Decision Log 276). O15 is done with it.
